Implement the following plan:

# SurrealDB Health Check & Recovery Plan

## Context

The vault's SurrealDB graph system has been down for ~22 hours. Two services are failing:
1. `cohezion-vault.service` (Cloud Vault MCP, port 8360) — crashes immediately on import: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'psutil'`
2. `cohezion-vault-sync.service` — restart-looping 7882+ times with `ERROR: SurrealDB not reachable on port 8001`

Root cause: The `surreal` binary is not in PATH and no SurrealDB process is running. The data files exist and are intact at `~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb` (RocksDB SST files, last written Mar 19 00:12).

## Diagnosis Summary

| Component | Status | Port |
|-----------|--------|------|
| SurrealDB server | DOWN — binary not in PATH, no process | 8001 (vault scripts) |
| Cloud Vault MCP | FAILED — `psutil` missing in venv | 8360 |
| Vault sync daemon | CRASH-LOOPING (7882 restarts) | — |
| Vault graph data | INTACT — RocksDB files present | — |
| Graph briefing | STALE — last generated Mar 18 23:01 | — |

## Fix Plan

### Step 1 — Install SurrealDB binary

```bash
curl -sSf https://install.surrealdb.com | sh
# Installs to ~/.surrealdb/surreal or ~/.local/bin/surreal
# Verify:
surreal version
```

### Step 2 — Start SurrealDB server

```bash
surreal start \
  --auth --user root --pass root \
  --bind 0.0.0.0:8001 \
  rocksdb://~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb &

sleep 2
curl -s -u root:root http://localhost:8001/health
```

### Step 3 — Verify data integrity

```bash
curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
  -u root:root \
  --data-raw 'SELECT count() FROM neuron GROUP ALL; SELECT count() FROM synapse GROUP ALL;'
```

Expected: ~1578 neurons, ~6203 synapses (matching last graph briefing).

### Step 4 — Fix psutil in cloud-vault-mcp

```bash
cd /home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/cloud-vault-mcp
uv add psutil
# or if using pip in the venv:
uv run pip install psutil
```

### Step 5 — Reset and restart services

```bash
# Reset restart counter for cohezion-vault (hit burst limit)
systemctl --user reset-failed cohezion-vault.service
systemctl --user start cohezion-vault.service

# Verify cohezion-vault is up
curl -s http://localhost:8360/health | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['status'])"

# vault-sync will recover automatically once SurrealDB is up (already in restart loop)
# Optionally reset its counter too:
systemctl --user reset-failed cohezion-vault-sync.service
```

### Step 6 — Regenerate graph briefing

```bash
cd /home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault
python3 scripts/graph_context.py briefing > /tmp/briefing_check.txt
head -10 /tmp/briefing_check.txt
```

## Verification

- [ ] `curl -s -u root:root http://localhost:8001/health` → `{"code":200,"details":"Check successful"}`
- [ ] Neuron count ~1578, synapse count ~6203
- [ ] `curl -s http://localhost:8360/health` returns `"status": "healthy"` (or `"degraded"` at worst)
- [ ] `systemctl --user status cohezion-vault-sync.service` shows active (running), not crash-looping
- [ ] `graph_context.py briefing` returns vault overview without errors

## Key Files

- `~/.config/systemd/user/cohezion-vault.service` — Cloud Vault MCP service (port 8360)
- `~/.config/systemd/user/cohezion-vault-sync.service` — Vault sync daemon
- `~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb/` — RocksDB data files (intact)
- `scripts/graph_context.py` — Graph query tool (uses port 8001)
- `scripts/triune-import.py` — Vault import (uses port 8001)
- `scripts/vault_sync.py` — Sync daemon (uses port 8001)
- `cloud-vault-mcp/src/mcp_server/health.py` — Imports psutil (line 12)

The `surreal` binary already exists at `~/.surrealdb/surreal`. Skipping installation — moving directly to starting the server.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
SurrealDB installs to `~/.surrealdb/` by default, outside the standard PATH. This is why the plan said "binary not in PATH" — it exists but wasn't discoverable via `which surreal`. We use the full path to invoke it.

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
SurrealDB v3 changed the auth model: authentication is **on by default** (no `--auth` flag needed). Use `--unauthenticated` to disable it. The `--user`/`--pass` aliases still work for root credentials.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

Now starting SurrealDB with the correct v3 flags:

> AGENT

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
The service uses a dedicated non-root user (`sdb_admin_session43`) — a security best practice. Root credentials start the DB server; application credentials authenticate at the namespace/database level. These are different concepts in SurrealDB's layered auth model.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

The service is missing `SURREALDB_URL=http://localhost:8001` — it's defaulting to port 8000. Adding it:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_016WBT1G4aUxUbwEcp8t7szk
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/mike-anderson/.config/systemd/user/cohezion-vault.service",
  "old_string": "Environment=SURREALDB_USERNAME=sdb_admin_session43\nREDACTED",
  "new_string": "Environment=SURREALDB_URL=http://localhost:8001\nEnvironment=SURREALDB_USERNAME=sdb_admin_session43\nREDACTED"
}
```

> AGENT

All systems restored. Summary of what was done and current state:

---

## Recovery Complete ✓

| Verification Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `SurrealDB /health` | HTTP 200 OK |
| Neuron count | 1578 (matches expected) |
| Synapse count | 8,805 (was 6,203 at last briefing — vault sync is catching up) |
| Cloud Vault MCP (`/health`) | **healthy** — all checks ok |
| SurrealDB subsystem in MCP | **ok** |
| Vault sync daemon | **active (running)** |
| Graph briefing | Regenerated successfully |

### What Was Fixed

1. **SurrealDB** — Binary already existed at `~/.surrealdb/surreal` (v3.0.0). Started it with correct v3 flags (`--username`/`--password` instead of `--auth`) pointing at the intact RocksDB data files.

2. **psutil missing** — Added `psutil>=5.9.0` to `pyproject.toml` and installed it into the venv. `health.py:12` was importing it without it being declared as a dependency.

3. **Wrong port in MCP service** — `cohezion-vault.service` had no `SURREALDB_URL` set, so it defaulted to port 8000. Added `Environment=SURREALDB_URL=http://localhost:8001` to the service file.

4. **Services restarted** — Reset failed counters, reloaded daemon, restarted both services.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
**Root cause chain:** SurrealDB stopped → sync daemon crash-looped → MCP crashed on import (unrelated `psutil` bug surfaced because the service had never successfully started before this session). Three independent failures, one triggering event. The port mismatch (`8000` default vs `8001` actual) was a latent config bug masked by the crash.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

**One remaining note:** SurrealDB was started manually as a background process. If the machine reboots, it won't auto-start. You may want to create a systemd user service for SurrealDB itself to make this persistent.

> AGENT

All three services active and fully healthy.

`★ Insight ─────────────────────────────────────`
Using `Wants=` rather than `Requires=` for the dependency means the dependent services will still start even if SurrealDB fails — they just won't have DB access. `Requires=` would stop the entire chain on DB failure, which is harsher but ensures no "zombie" services running without their dependency. Given the sync daemon's crash-loop history, `Wants=` is the safer choice here — it degrades gracefully rather than going dark.
`─────────────────────────────────────────────────`

### What was created

**`~/.config/systemd/user/surrealdb.service`** — new service, enabled, starts on login:
- Runs `surreal start` with the RocksDB data path
- `Restart=on-failure` with 5s backoff
- Logs to journal (`journalctl --user -u surrealdb.service`)

**`cohezion-vault.service`** and **`cohezion-vault-sync.service`** — both now declare `After=surrealdb.service` and `Wants=surrealdb.service`, so systemd starts SurrealDB first on login before these services come up.

> AGENT

Evaluating the session for extractable knowledge...

**Self-evaluation:**
1. SurrealDB v3 removed `--auth` flag (auth is on by default now) — not obvious from v2 docs
2. Binary installs to `~/.surrealdb/` (outside PATH) — easy to miss
3. Three-failure root cause chain: DB down → sync loop → MCP crash on import (unrelated `psutil` bug)
4. Latent port mismatch: MCP defaults to 8000, vault uses 8001 — masked until first restart
5. Systemd `Wants=` vs `Requires=` tradeoff for degraded-mode tolerance

This is a clear "repeatable workflow + non-obvious discoveries" case — worth extracting.

```
     1→---
     2→name: surrealdb-debug
     3→description: |
     4→  Diagnose and fix common SurrealDB failures in the Cohezion vault.
     5→  Use when: (1) HTTP 401 Unauthorized, (2) "Couldn't write to a read only transaction",
     6→  (3) SurrealDB systemd service is failed, (4) schema errors on INFO FOR DB or DEFINE,
     7→  (5) records are silently not persisting after CREATE/UPSERT (SCHEMAFULL silent rejection),
     8→  (6) neuron paths are stale after vault directory renames,
     9→  (7) synapse count doubles on re-import, (8) ID collisions after re-import with different derivation,
    10→  (9) wrong aspect field (all neurons getting 'connective' default due to stale DIR_TO_ASPECT mapping),
    11→  (10) ORDER BY inside DEFINE FUNCTION doesn't sort — results come back in arbitrary order,
    12→  (11) "in" reserved keyword conflict — SELECT in FROM synapse fails with parse error,
    13→  (12) count() GROUP result is wrapped — returns [{count: N}] not N,
    14→  (13) N+1 query: searching N candidates with N sequential HTTP calls — batch into
    15→  one OR query for 3-10x speedup,
    16→  (14) _esc() for SurrealQL string literals must escape backslashes BEFORE single
    17→  quotes, or a trailing backslash breaks the string boundary.
    18→  Key insight: credentials and port in docs are often stale — always inspect the
    19→  live process, and always create namespace+database before applying schema.
    20→author: Claude Code
    21→version: 1.7.0
    22→---
    23→
    24→# SurrealDB Debugging
    25→
    26→## Problem
    27→
    28→SurrealDB connections fail with authentication errors, read-only transaction errors,
    29→or schema application failures. Documented credentials are stale. The systemd service
    30→may be failed while a manual process runs on a different port.
    31→
    32→## Step 1: Find the Live Process
    33→
    34→Never trust documented credentials. Always inspect what's actually running:
    35→
    36→```bash
    37→ps aux | grep surreal | grep -v grep
    38→```
    39→
    40→This reveals the actual `--user`, `--pass`, `--bind` port, and `--path` used.
    41→
    42→Example output:
    43→```
    44→surreal start --auth --user root --pass root --bind 0.0.0.0:8001 rocksdb://path/to/db
    45→```
    46→
    47→## Step 2: Test Connectivity
    48→
    49→```bash
    50→curl -s -u root:root http://localhost:8001/health
    51→# or
    52→curl -s http://localhost:8001/health  # if no auth
    53→```
    54→
    55→If connection refused: the process died or is on a different port. Check the port
    56→from `ps aux` output above.
    57→
    58→## Step 3: Fix "read only transaction" Errors
    59→
    60→This error occurs when you try to run statements (even INFO FOR DB) before the
    61→namespace and database exist. Fix: create them explicitly first.
    62→
    63→```bash
    64→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
    65→  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    66→  -H "NS: cohezion" -H "DB: vault" \
    67→  -u root:root \
    68→  --data-raw 'DEFINE NAMESPACE cohezion; USE NS cohezion; DEFINE DATABASE vault;'
    69→```
    70→
    71→Then verify the schema can be queried:
    72→```bash
    73→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
    74→  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
    75→  -H "NS: cohezion" -H "DB: vault" \
    76→  -u root:root \
    77→  --data-raw 'INFO FOR DB;'
    78→```
    79→
    80→## Step 4: Apply Schema
    81→
    82→After namespace+database exist:
    83→
    84→```bash
    85→surreal import --conn http://localhost:8001 --ns cohezion --db vault \
    86→  -u root -p root scripts/triune-schema.surql
    87→```
    88→
    89→Or via HTTP:
    90→```bash
    91→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
    92→  -H "NS: cohezion" -H "DB: vault" \
    93→  -u root:root \
    94→  --data-raw "$(cat scripts/triune-schema.surql)"
    95→```
    96→
    97→## Step 5: Start Fresh Instance (if systemd service is broken)
    98→
    99→```bash
   100→mkdir -p ~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb
   101→
   102→surreal start \
   103→  --auth --user root --pass root \
   104→  --bind 0.0.0.0:8001 \
   105→  rocksdb://~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb &
   106→
   107→# Wait for it to be ready
   108→sleep 2
   109→curl -s -u root:root http://localhost:8001/health
   110→```
   111→
   112→## Batch Import Fallback Pattern
   113→
   114→When importing large volumes via SQL, batch statements fail when any single one has
   115→a parse error (bad characters in note titles, etc.). Fall back to one-at-a-time:
   116→
   117→```python
   118→def batch_query(self, statements: list[str], batch_size: int = 50) -> int:
   119→    success = 0
   120→    for i in range(0, len(statements), batch_size):
   121→        batch = statements[i:i + batch_size]
   122→        combined = "\n".join(batch)
   123→        results = self.query(combined)
   124→        # If batch returns single ERR, fall back to one-at-a-time
   125→        if len(results) == 1 and results[0].get("status") == "ERR":
   126→            for stmt in batch:
   127→                r = self.query(stmt)
   128→                if r and r[0].get("status") == "OK":
   129→                    success += 1
   130→        else:
   131→            success += sum(1 for r in results if r.get("status") == "OK")
   132→    return success
   133→```
   134→
   135→## Vault-Specific Configuration
   136→
   137→| Setting | Value |
   138→|---------|-------|
   139→| Port | 8001 |
   140→| Auth | root / root |
   141→| Namespace | cohezion |
   142→| Database | vault |
   143→| Storage | rocksdb at `~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb` |
   144→| Schema | `scripts/triune-schema.surql` |
   145→| Import script | `scripts/triune-import.py` |
   146→
   147→## RELATE vs CREATE for Relation Tables
   148→
   149→Tables with `in TYPE record<X>` and `out TYPE record<Y>` are **RELATION tables**.
   150→You MUST use `RELATE` syntax, not `CREATE`:
   151→
   152→```sql
   153→-- WRONG (returns "expected a RELATION IN neuron OUT neuron" error)
   154→CREATE kinship CONTENT { in: neuron:foo, out: neuron:bar, relation: "moiety" };
   155→
   156→-- CORRECT
   157→RELATE neuron:foo->kinship->neuron:bar CONTENT { relation: "moiety", obligation: "cite together" };
   158→```
   159→
   160→**How to detect:** If a CREATE on a table with `in`/`out` record fields fails with
   161→"which is not a relation, but expected a RELATION IN X OUT Y", switch to RELATE.
   162→
   163→## SCHEMAFULL Silent Record Rejection
   164→
   165→**SCHEMAFULL tables silently discard records with unknown fields — no error is returned.**
   166→
   167→This is the hardest SurrealDB bug to diagnose: `SELECT count()` returns 0 after what
   168→appeared to be a successful INSERT/UPSERT, but there's no error message.
   169→
   170→**Diagnosis:** Add an unknown field to a CREATE statement and check if it persists:
   171→```bash
   172→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   173→  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   174→  -u root:root \
   175→  --data-raw 'CREATE country:test CONTENT { name: "test", mystery_field: "x" };
   176→              SELECT * FROM country:test;'
   177→# If mystery_field is absent from the result → table is SCHEMAFULL
   178→```
   179→
   180→**Fix:** Add the missing field to the schema:
   181→```bash
   182→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   183→  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   184→  -u root:root \
   185→  --data-raw 'DEFINE FIELD avg_activation ON country TYPE float DEFAULT 0.5;
   186→              DEFINE FIELD updated ON country TYPE string DEFAULT "";'
   187→```
   188→
   189→**Common culprits in Triune Vault:**
   190→- `country` table: `avg_activation`, `updated` fields were added later
   191→- `hiho_event` table: `country` field type changed from `record<country>` to `string`
   192→- After any schema change, re-run the import script
   193→
   194→## Stale Neuron Paths After Directory Renames
   195→
   196→When the vault directories were renamed (e.g., `concepts/` → `cortex/`) AFTER the
   197→SurrealDB import ran, all neuron paths become stale. Queries by path return nothing.
   198→
   199→**Diagnose:**
   200→```bash
   201→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   202→  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   203→  -u root:root \
   204→  --data-raw "SELECT count() FROM neuron WHERE string::starts_with(path, 'concepts/') GROUP ALL;"
   205→# If > 0, paths are stale
   206→```
   207→
   208→**Fix — batch rename (SurrealDB 3.0 uses `string::starts_with`, NOT `STARTS WITH`):**
   209→```bash
   210→RENAMES=(
   211→  "concepts cortex"
   212→  "papers sensory"
   213→  "lessons memory"
   214→  "specs genome"
   215→  "decisions prefrontal"
   216→  "experiments laboratory"
   217→  "patterns cerebellum"
   218→  "projects motor"
   219→  "inbox thalamus"
   220→  "canvas visual-cortex"
   221→)
   222→
   223→for pair in "${RENAMES[@]}"; do
   224→  old=$(echo $pair | cut -d' ' -f1)
   225→  new=$(echo $pair | cut -d' ' -f2)
   226→  curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   227→    -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   228→    -u root:root \
   229→    --data-raw "UPDATE neuron SET path = string::replace(path, '${old}/', '${new}/') WHERE string::starts_with(path, '${old}/');"
   230→done
   231→```
   232→
   233→Apply same pattern to `cluster_id` field if it also stores directory names.
   234→
   235→## json.dumps ensure_ascii=False for SurrealQL
   236→
   237→**Python's `json.dumps()` defaults to `ensure_ascii=True`, encoding emoji/Unicode as `\uXXXX` escape sequences. SurrealDB rejects these with HTTP 400.**
   238→
   239→```python
   240→# WRONG — SurrealDB returns 400 Bad Request
   241→title = "🏆 PROJECT COMPLETE"
   242→sql = f'UPDATE neuron SET title = {json.dumps(title)};'
   243→# Produces: title = "\ud83c\udfc6 PROJECT COMPLETE"  ← surrogate pair, SurrealDB can't parse
   244→
   245→# CORRECT
   246→sql = f'UPDATE neuron SET title = {json.dumps(title, ensure_ascii=False)};'
   247→# Produces: title = "🏆 PROJECT COMPLETE"  ← raw UTF-8, SurrealDB accepts
   248→```
   249→
   250→**Rule:** Always use `json.dumps(value, ensure_ascii=False)` when building SurrealQL strings.
   251→
   252→## Field Projection HTTP 400
   253→
   254→**SurrealDB 3.0 returns HTTP 400 for field-projected queries on some tables.** This is silent and confusing — the same query with `SELECT *` works fine.
   255→
   256→**Symptom:** `SELECT country, coherence_score FROM hiho_event ORDER BY date DESC` returns HTTP 400, but `SELECT * FROM hiho_event` works.
   257→
   258→**Affected patterns:**
   259→- Projected fields on tables with computed/derived columns
   260→- `GROUP BY` on RELATION tables (kinship, synapse): `SELECT out, count() FROM kinship GROUP BY out` → HTTP 400
   261→- Any field-projected query that SurrealDB's query planner can't resolve
   262→
   263→**Fix: Use `SELECT *` and filter client-side:**
   264→```python
   265→# WRONG — HTTP 400
   266→results = query("SELECT country, coherence_score FROM hiho_event ORDER BY date DESC;")
   267→
   268→# CORRECT — fetch all, filter in Python
   269→all_events = query("SELECT * FROM hiho_event;")
   270→results = sorted(all_events, key=lambda e: e.get("date",""), reverse=True)
   271→hiho_scores = {e["country"]: e["coherence_score"] for e in all_events if "country" in e}
   272→```
   273→
   274→**GROUP BY alternative (for relation tables like kinship, synapse):**
   275→```python
   276→# WRONG — HTTP 400
   277→counts = query("SELECT out AS nid, count() FROM kinship GROUP BY out;")
   278→
   279→# CORRECT — fetch all, aggregate in Python
   280→all_kin = query("SELECT in, out FROM kinship;")
   281→kinship_counts = {}
   282→for r in all_kin:
   283→    for field in ["in", "out"]:
   284→        nid = str(r.get(field, ""))
   285→        kinship_counts[nid] = kinship_counts.get(nid, 0) + 1
   286→```
   287→
   288→## Neuron IDs Are Immutable (Use Path Field for Lookups)
   289→
   290→**SurrealDB record IDs are immutable after creation.** After vault directory renames (e.g., `concepts/` → `cortex/`), neuron IDs still contain the OLD directory name even after path fields are updated.
   291→
   292→**Example:**
   293→```
   294→# ID (immutable, reflects import-time path):
   295→neuron:concepts_compound_engineering_md
   296→
   297→# path field (updated by UPDATE SET path = ...):
   298→cortex/compound-engineering.md
   299→```
   300→
   301→**Consequence:** If you derive an ID from a current vault path, it won't match the stored ID.
   302→
   303→**Always query by path, never by derived ID:**
   304→```python
   305→# WRONG — ID derived from current path won't match
   306→nid = "neuron:" + path.replace("/", "_").replace("-", "_").replace(".md", "_md")
   307→result = query(f"SELECT * FROM ONLY {nid};")  # → NONE
   308→
   309→# CORRECT — query by path field
   310→result = query(f'SELECT * FROM neuron WHERE path = "{path}" LIMIT 1;')
   311→```
   312→
   313→**Verification:**
   314→```bash
   315→# Find neurons where ID and path disagree
   316→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   317→  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   318→  -u root:root \
   319→  --data-raw 'SELECT id, path FROM neuron WHERE NOT string::contains(string::lowercase(string::slice(string::from(id), 7)), string::replace(string::split(path, "/")[1], "-", "_")) LIMIT 5;'
   320→```
   321→
   322→## Graph-Walk Trajectory Generation (Synthetic Training Data)
   323→
   324→When temporal history is unavailable (all records have same timestamp from bulk import), use SurrealDB's graph traversal to generate synthetic trajectories for ML training:
   325→
   326→```python
   327→def extract_graph_walk_trajectories(neuron_vectors, n_walks=200, walk_length=8):
   328→    """Generate random walks using SurrealDB ->synapse->neuron traversal."""
   329→    seeds = query(
   330→        "SELECT id, cluster_id FROM neuron WHERE activation > 0.4 "
   331→        "AND synapse_out > 2 ORDER BY RAND() LIMIT 200;"
   332→    )
   333→    trajectories = []
   334→    for seed in seeds[:n_walks]:
   335→        walk = [seed["id"]]
   336→        current = seed["id"]
   337→        for _ in range(walk_length - 1):
   338→            nbrs_result = query(f"SELECT ->synapse->neuron AS n FROM {current};")
   339→            if not nbrs_result:
   340→                break
   341→            nbrs = nbrs_result[0].get("n", [])
   342→            if not nbrs:
   343→                break
   344→            current = random.choice(nbrs)
   345→            if str(current) in [str(w) for w in walk]:
   346→                break  # avoid cycles
   347→            walk.append(current)
   348→        if len(walk) >= 3:
   349→            # Convert to 12D vector sequence
   350→            vecs = [neuron_vectors[str(nid)] for nid in walk if str(nid) in neuron_vectors]
   351→            if len(vecs) >= 3:
   352→                trajectories.append({"waypoints": [str(n) for n in walk], "vectors": vecs})
   353→    return trajectories
   354→```
   355→
   356→Key: `SELECT ->synapse->neuron AS n FROM {node_id}` returns all outbound neighbors in one hop. This is SurrealDB's graph traversal syntax — no JOIN needed.
   357→
   358→## Import Idempotency: Synapses Double on Re-Import
   359→
   360→**Symptom:** Synapse count doubles each time `triune-import.py` runs (e.g., 8,658 → 19,578 → 39,156).
   361→
   362→**Cause:** `RELATE` for synapse/kinship tables creates new records even if the same relationship already exists. There is no upsert for relation tables.
   363→
   364→**Fix:** DELETE all synapses before re-creating them in the import script:
   365→
   366→```python
   367→# Phase 2 — synapse import (idempotent)
   368→print("Deleting existing synapses before re-import...")
   369→db.query("DELETE synapse;")  # Must come BEFORE re-creating synapses
   370→
   371→# Then create synapses as usual
   372→for source_path, targets in links.items():
   373→    ...
   374→    db.query(f"RELATE {src_id}->synapse->{tgt_id} CONTENT {{...}};")
   375→```
   376→
   377→Same pattern applies to `kinship` and any other relation table.
   378→
   379→## Import Idempotency: ID Collisions After Re-Import
   380→
   381→**Symptom:** Import logs show errors like `neuron:cortex_moc_triune_self_md already exists with different ID`. Neurons accumulate stale duplicates.
   382→
   383→**Cause:** The ID derivation algorithm may differ between import runs (e.g., if the path-to-ID function was changed). SurrealDB IDs are immutable — the old record can't be updated with a new ID.
   384→
   385→**Fix: Phase 0 — resolve ID collisions before import:**
   386→
   387→```python
   388→# Phase 0: Build map of existing path → ID from the live database
   389→existing = db.query("SELECT id, path FROM neuron")
   390→path_to_existing_id: dict[str, str] = {}
   391→if existing and existing[0].get("result"):
   392→    for row in existing[0]["result"]:
   393→        raw_id = str(row["id"]).removeprefix("neuron:")
   394→        path_to_existing_id[row["path"]] = raw_id
   395→
   396→# Override record_id for any note that already exists under a different ID
   397→for note in notes:
   398→    if note["path"] in path_to_existing_id:
   399→        existing_id = path_to_existing_id[note["path"]]
   400→        if existing_id != note["record_id"]:
   401→            note["record_id"] = existing_id  # Reuse the old ID for UPSERT
   402→```
   403→
   404→This ensures UPSERT uses the existing record's ID rather than generating a new one.
   405→
   406→## Import Idempotency: Orphan Cleanup
   407→
   408→After directory renames or file deletions, old neuron records accumulate for files that no longer exist.
   409→
   410→**Fix: Phase 5 — delete neurons whose vault files are gone:**
   411→
   412→```python
   413→# Phase 5: Orphan neuron cleanup
   414→all_neurons = db.query("SELECT id, path FROM neuron")
   415→if all_neurons and all_neurons[0].get("result"):
   416→    vault_paths = {n["path"] for n in notes}  # paths computed during import
   417→    for row in all_neurons[0]["result"]:
   418→        if row["path"] not in vault_paths:
   419→            nid = str(row["id"])
   420→            db.query(f"DELETE {nid};")
   421→            print(f"  Deleted orphan neuron: {nid}")
   422→```
   423→
   424→## Wrong Aspect Mapping (All Neurons Get 'connective')
   425→
   426→**Symptom:** After import, aspect distribution shows ~70% `connective` (the default fallback). Expected: knower~30%, thinker~22%, doer~44%, connective~4%.
   427→
   428→**Cause:** `DIR_TO_ASPECT` in `triune-import.py` uses old/pre-rename directory names that no longer match the vault's current structure.
   429→
   430→**Fix:** Update `DIR_TO_ASPECT` to include ALL current directory names:
   431→
   432→```python
   433→DIR_TO_ASPECT = {
   434→    # Knower (ground truth)
   435→    "cortex": "knower",       # was: concepts
   436→    "sensory": "knower",      # was: papers
   437→    "memory": "knower",       # was: lessons
   438→    "genome": "knower",       # was: specs
   439→    # Thinker (reasoning)
   440→    "prefrontal": "thinker",  # was: decisions
   441→    "laboratory": "thinker",  # was: experiments
   442→    "cerebellum": "thinker",  # was: patterns
   443→    # Doer (action)
   444→    "motor": "doer",          # was: projects
   445→    "hippocampus": "doer",    # was: daily/sessions
   446→    "thalamus": "doer",       # was: inbox
   447→    "missions": "doer",
   448→    "retrospectives": "doer",
   449→    "Agents": "doer",
   450→    # Connective
   451→    "dreaming": "connective",
   452→    "songlines": "connective",
   453→    "subconscious": "connective",
   454→    "metabolism": "connective",
   455→    "visual-cortex": "connective",
   456→}
   457→```
   458→
   459→**Sanity check after import:**
   460→```bash
   461→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   462→  -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" \
   463→  -u root:root \
   464→  --data-raw 'SELECT aspect, count() FROM neuron GROUP BY aspect;'
   465→# Expected: connective < 10% of total
   466→```
   467→
   468→## ORDER BY Inside DEFINE FUNCTION Doesn't Sort (SurrealDB 3.0)
   469→
   470→**Symptom:** `LET $top = (SELECT ... ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 5)` inside a function returns arbitrary records, not the top-N by activation. The exact same query works correctly as an ad-hoc SQL call.
   471→
   472→**Cause:** SurrealDB 3.0 does not preserve `ORDER BY` on `LET` variable assignments inside `DEFINE FUNCTION` bodies. This is a known limitation — the query planner does not push ordering through the function's variable scope.
   473→
   474→**Fix:** Issue a direct query from the client for any results that need correct ordering. Don't rely on ordering inside function bodies.
   475→
   476→```python
   477→# In graph_context.py — neighborhood command
   478→sql = f"SELECT * FROM fn::context_neighborhood({nid});"
   479→data = query(sql)[0]["result"][0]
   480→
   481→# Workaround: fetch cluster_top via direct query (function ordering is broken)
   482→cluster_sql = (
   483→    f"SELECT id, title, activation, stage FROM neuron "
   484→    f"WHERE cluster_id = '{cluster_id}' AND id != {nid} "
   485→    f"ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 5;"
   486→)
   487→data["cluster_top"] = query(cluster_sql)[0]["result"]
   488→```
   489→
   490→**Rule of thumb:** Use SurrealDB functions for data *retrieval* and *aggregation*. Use direct queries for anything that needs reliable *ordering*.
   491→
   492→## `in` Is a Reserved Keyword in SurrealQL
   493→
   494→**Symptom:** Queries on RELATION tables (synapse, kinship) fail with a parse error when selecting the `in` field directly.
   495→
   496→```sql
   497→-- FAILS — parse error
   498→SELECT DISTINCT in AS id FROM synapse WHERE in.cluster_id = "cortex";
   499→
   500→-- WORKS — use VALUE to extract the field
   501→SELECT VALUE in FROM synapse WHERE in = neuron:foo;
   502→
   503→-- WORKS — use field access via traversal
   504→SELECT in.id, in.title FROM synapse WHERE out = neuron:bar;
   505→```
   506→
   507→**Context:** `in` and `out` are SurrealDB reserved keywords for RELATION table edge fields. They cannot be aliased or selected with bare `SELECT in` syntax. Use `SELECT VALUE in` (returns an array of raw values) or `SELECT in.field` (dot-access for nested fields).
   508→
   509→## count() GROUP Result Is Wrapped
   510→
   511→**Symptom:** `SELECT count() FROM neuron GROUP ALL` returns `[{'count': 1602}]` instead of `1602`. Code that expects a plain integer breaks.
   512→
   513→**Pattern:**
   514→
   515→```python
   516→def _unwrap_count(v):
   517→    """Unwrap count() GROUP result: [{count: N}] → N."""
   518→    if isinstance(v, list) and v and isinstance(v[0], dict):
   519→        return v[0].get("count", v)
   520→    return v
   521→
   522→# Usage
   523→result = query("SELECT count() FROM neuron GROUP ALL;")
   524→total = _unwrap_count(result[0]["result"])  # → 1602 (int)
   525→```
   526→
   527→**Why this happens:** `GROUP ALL` always returns a list with one aggregated row. The `count()` value lives at `result[0]["result"][0]["count"]`. Using `SELECT VALUE count() FROM neuron GROUP ALL` returns the same wrapped format.
   528→
   529→## N+1 Query: Batch Multiple Title Searches into One OR Query
   530→
   531→**Symptom:** A hook or search function issues N sequential HTTP requests to SurrealDB
   532→to find the first matching record across N candidate strings. At 12 candidates with
   533→a 3s timeout each, worst-case latency is 36 seconds. Even at normal speed (15ms/query),
   534→12 queries = 180ms vs 1 batch = 25ms.
   535→
   536→**Anti-pattern (sequential):**
   537→```python
   538→for candidate in candidates:
   539→    sql = f"SELECT ... FROM neuron WHERE string::contains(title, '{candidate}') LIMIT 1;"
   540→    result = query(sql)
   541→    if result: return result[0]
   542→```
   543→
   544→**Fix — batch with OR conditions, filter client-side:**
   545→```python
   546→conditions = " OR ".join(
   547→    f"string::contains(string::lowercase(title), '{_esc(c)}')"
   548→    for c in candidates
   549→)
   550→sql = f"SELECT id, title, activation, path FROM neuron WHERE {conditions} ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 20;"
   551→hits = query(sql)[0]["result"]
   552→
   553→# Preserve candidate priority order client-side
   554→for candidate in candidates:       # earlier candidates = higher priority
   555→    for hit in hits:
   556→        if candidate in hit.get("title", "").lower():
   557→            return hit
   558→return hits[0] if hits else None   # fallback: highest activation
   559→```
   560→
   561→**Key insight:** One round-trip with client-side filtering beats N round-trips by
   562→3-10x in practice. The ORDER BY activation gives a quality fallback when priority
   563→ordering doesn't find a distinct winner.
   564→
   565→## SurrealQL String Escaping: Backslash Before Single Quote
   566→
   567→**Symptom:** Notes with backslash in the title (e.g., `C:\path` or `a\b`) produce
   568→malformed SurrealQL. If input contains `\`, the naive `_esc` transforms `foo\'` into
   569→`'foo\''` — the backslash escapes the closing quote, breaking the query.
   570→
   571→**Broken pattern:**
   572→```python
   573→def _esc(s):
   574→    return s.replace("'", "\\'")   # WRONG — backslash not escaped first
   575→```
   576→
   577→**Example failure:**
   578→```
   579→Input:  "foo\"
   580→After _esc: "foo\"
   581→SQL:    WHERE title = 'foo\'   ← backslash eats the closing quote → broken
   582→```
   583→
   584→**Fix — always escape backslash BEFORE single quote:**
   585→```python
   586→def _esc(s: str) -> str:
   587→    return s.replace("\\", "\\\\").replace("'", "\\'")
   588→```
   589→
   590→**Rule:** In SurrealQL string literals, backslash is the escape character. You must
   591→double it (`\\`) before handling any other escaped characters. This is the same rule
   592→as MySQL, SQLite, and most SQL dialects. Apply to ALL user-controlled string
   593→interpolation into SurrealQL queries.
   594→
   595→## Verification
   596→
   597→```bash
   598→# Check tables exist
   599→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   600→  -H "NS: cohezion" -H "DB: vault" \
   601→  -u root:root \
   602→  --data-raw 'INFO FOR DB;' | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(list(d[0]['result']['tables'].keys()))"
   603→
   604→# Check record counts
   605→curl -s -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql \
   606→  -H "NS: cohezion" -H "DB: vault" \
   607→  -u root:root \
   608→  --data-raw 'SELECT count() FROM neuron GROUP ALL; SELECT count() FROM synapse GROUP ALL;'
   609→```
   610→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

  "new_string": "## Step 5: Start Fresh Instance (if systemd service is broken)\n\n**Binary is at `~/.surrealdb/surreal` — not in PATH. Use full path.**\n\n**SurrealDB v3 change:** `--auth` flag removed. Auth is ON by default. Use `--username`/`--password` (or `--user`/`--pass` aliases). Do NOT include `--auth`.\n\n```bash\nmkdir -p ~/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb\n\n~/.surrealdb/surreal start \\\n  --username root --password root \\\n  --bind 0.0.0.0:8001 \\\n  --no-banner \\\n  rocksdb:///home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb &\n\n# Wait for it to be ready\nsleep 3\ncurl -s -u root:root http://localhost:8001/health\n# Returns: HTTP 200 with empty body (healthy)\n```\n\n**v2 vs v3 flag comparison:**\n```\n# v2 (BROKEN in v3)\nsurreal start --auth --user root --pass root ...\n\n# v3 (correct)\n~/.surrealdb/surreal start --username root --password root ...\n```\n\n## Setting Up Persistent systemd Service\n\nTo survive reboots, create `~/.config/systemd/user/surrealdb.service`:\n\n```ini\n[Unit]\nDescription=SurrealDB Server (Cohezion Vault)\nAfter=network.target\nStartLimitBurst=5\nStartLimitIntervalSec=60\n\n[Service]\nType=simple\nExecStart=/home/mike-anderson/.surrealdb/surreal start \\\n    --username root \\\n    --password root \\\n    --bind 0.0.0.0:8001 \\\n    --no-banner \\\n    rocksdb:///home/mike-anderson/dev/cohezion/data/surrealdb\nRestart=on-failure\nRestartSec=5\nStandardOutput=journal\nStandardError=journal\n\n[Install]\nWantedBy=default.target\n```\n\nAdd `After=surrealdb.service` and `Wants=surrealdb.service` to dependent services:\n\n```ini\n# In cohezion-vault.service and cohezion-vault-sync.service:\n[Unit]\nAfter=network.target surrealdb.service\nWants=surrealdb.service\n```\n\nThen enable and start:\n```bash\nsystemctl --user daemon-reload\nsystemctl --user enable --now surrealdb.service\nsystemctl --user status surrealdb.service\n```\n\n**`Wants=` vs `Requires=`:** Use `Wants=` so dependent services still attempt to start\neven if SurrealDB fails (degraded mode). Use `Requires=` for hard dependency (chain stops\nif DB fails).\n\n## cohezion-vault.service Port Mismatch\n\nThe MCP service defaults `SURREALDB_URL` to port **8000** but the vault uses port **8001**.\nIf SurrealDB health shows \"Connection refused\" despite SurrealDB running:\n\n```bash\n# Check what URL the service is using\ngrep -i surreal ~/.config/systemd/user/cohezion-vault.service\n\n# Add the missing env var\n# In cohezion-vault.service [Service] section:\nEnvironment=SURREALDB_URL=http://localhost:8001\n\n# Reload and restart\nsystemctl --user daemon-reload\nsystemctl --user restart cohezion-vault.service\ncurl -s http://localhost:8360/health | python3 -c \"import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print(d['checks']['surrealdb']['status'])\"\n```"
}

324 /home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/scripts/graph_context.py
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Graph Context — Token-efficient vault context via SurrealDB graph traversal.

Queries the vault's neuron/synapse graph and returns compact, agent-ready context.
Designed for minimal token consumption: one call replaces reading multiple files.

Usage:
    graph_context.py neighborhood <query>     # Local neighborhood of a neuron
    graph_context.py search <query>           # Find neurons by title/tag match
    graph_context.py cluster <name>           # Cluster summary with top neurons
    graph_context.py hops <query> [depth]     # N-hop traversal (default: 2)
    graph_context.py bridges <cluster_a> <cluster_b>  # Cross-domain connectors
    graph_context.py stats                    # Global vault health snapshot
    graph_context.py resolve <query>          # Find neuron ID from partial name
    graph_context.py briefing                  # Full vault briefing for agent context

Output is plain text, optimized for agent context injection.
"""

import base64
import json
import sys
import urllib.request

SURREAL_URL = "http://localhost:8001/sql"
SURREAL_NS = "cohezion"
SURREAL_DB = "vault"
SURREAL_USER = "root"
SURREAL_PASS = "root"


def query(sql: str) -> list:
    """Execute SurrealQL and return result list."""
    creds = base64.b64encode(f"{SURREAL_USER}:{SURREAL_PASS}".encode()).decode()
    req = urllib.request.Request(
        SURREAL_URL,
        data=sql.encode("utf-8"),
        headers={
            "Accept": "application/json",
            "Content-Type": "text/plain",
            "Authorization": f"Basic {creds}",
            "surreal-ns": SURREAL_NS,
            "surreal-db": SURREAL_DB,
        },
        method="POST",
    )
    with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as resp:
        data = json.loads(resp.read())
    if isinstance(data, dict) and "code" in data:
        print(f"Error: {data.get('description', data)}", file=sys.stderr)
        sys.exit(1)
    return data


def resolve_neuron(partial: str) -> str | None:
    """Find a neuron ID from a partial name match."""
    sql = f"SELECT id, title, activation FROM neuron WHERE string::contains(string::lowercase(title), string::lowercase('{_esc(partial)}')) ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 5;"
    results = query(sql)
    hits = results[0].get("result", [])
```

     1→---
     2→name: graph-context
     3→description: |
     4→  Query the vault's SurrealDB graph for token-efficient context.
     5→  Use when: (1) you need to understand what a note connects to,
     6→  (2) you want to find related concepts without reading files,
     7→  (3) you need cluster-level awareness of a domain,
     8→  (4) you want to find cross-domain bridge notes,
     9→  (5) you need a vault health snapshot.
    10→  Key insight: one graph query (~460 tokens) replaces reading 13 files (~39,500 tokens).
    11→  86x compression ratio. Not all tokens are created equal.
    12→  Automated layers: (6) briefing at session start (~910 tokens, pre-computed in
    13→  metabolism/graph-briefing.md), (7) per-prompt hook injects ~275 tokens of
    14→  graph context automatically via UserPromptSubmit hook.
    15→author: Claude Code
    16→version: 1.1.0
    17→---
    18→
    19→# Graph Context — Token-Efficient Vault Awareness
    20→
    21→## When to Use
    22→
    23→Before reading vault files to understand relationships, query the graph first.
    24→The graph tells you WHAT connects to WHAT and HOW STRONGLY — in 460 tokens
    25→instead of 39,500.
    26→
    27→| Situation | Command | Tokens Saved |
    28→|-----------|---------|-------------|
    29→| Understanding a note's connections | `neighborhood <name>` | ~9x vs reading the note |
    30→| Finding related concepts | `search <query>` | ~20x vs grepping files |
    31→| Domain overview | `cluster <name>` | ~50x vs reading all cluster files |
    32→| Cross-domain connections | `bridges <a> <b>` | No file equivalent |
    33→| Vault health check | `stats` | ~100x vs auditing manually |
    34→
    35→## Commands
    36→
    37→All commands run via `python3 scripts/graph_context.py <command> [args]`.
    38→
    39→### neighborhood (alias: n)
    40→
    41→Returns a neuron's metadata, all outbound/inbound connections with activation
    42→scores, and top cluster siblings.
    43→
    44→```bash
    45→python3 scripts/graph_context.py neighborhood sarfatti
    46→python3 scripts/graph_context.py n "exotic vacuum"
    47→```
    48→
    49→### search (alias: s)
    50→
    51→Finds neurons by title or tag substring match, sorted by activation.
    52→
    53→```bash
    54→python3 scripts/graph_context.py search quantum
    55→python3 scripts/graph_context.py s "back-reaction"
    56→```
    57→
    58→### cluster (alias: c)
    59→
    60→Returns cluster summary: neuron count, average activation, coherence score,
    61→and top 10 neurons.
    62→
    63→```bash
    64→python3 scripts/graph_context.py cluster cortex
    65→python3 scripts/graph_context.py c sensory
    66→```
    67→
    68→### hops (alias: h)
    69→
    70→N-hop graph traversal from a starting neuron. Default depth: 2.
    71→
    72→```bash
    73→python3 scripts/graph_context.py hops sarfatti 2
    74→python3 scripts/graph_context.py h "pilot wave" 1
    75→```
    76→
    77→### bridges (alias: b)
    78→
    79→Finds neurons that bridge two clusters — cross-domain connectors.
    80→
    81→```bash
    82→python3 scripts/graph_context.py bridges cortex sensory
    83→python3 scripts/graph_context.py b prefrontal laboratory
    84→```
    85→
    86→### stats
    87→
    88→Global vault health snapshot.
    89→
    90→```bash
    91→python3 scripts/graph_context.py stats
    92→```
    93→
    94→### resolve (alias: r)
    95→
    96→Find neuron IDs from partial name matches.
    97→
    98→```bash
    99→python3 scripts/graph_context.py resolve bohm
   100→```
   101→
   102→### briefing
   103→
   104→Compact vault overview for agent session-start context injection. Outputs:
   105→- Vitals (neuron/synapse counts, stage distribution)
   106→- Hot neurons (top 15 by activation)
   107→- Cross-domain bridges (top 10 by connectivity)
   108→- Attention needed (embryos, orphans)
   109→- Highest energy (activation ≥ 0.9)
   110→
   111→```bash
   112→python3 scripts/graph_context.py briefing
   113→# Used by cron to pre-compute:
   114→# python3 scripts/graph_context.py briefing > metabolism/graph-briefing.md
   115→```
   116→
   117→**Pre-computed version:** `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` (~910 tokens) is regenerated
   118→by `scripts/dreaming-cron.sh` after each dreaming engine run. Read this at session
   119→start instead of running the command live.
   120→
   121→## Automated Context Injection (Hook Layer)
   122→
   123→Two automated layers run without any explicit command:
   124→
   125→### Layer 1: Session-Start Briefing
   126→
   127→`metabolism/graph-briefing.md` is injected as startup context via CLAUDE.md directive.
   128→~910 tokens, pre-computed by cron.
   129→
   130→### Layer 2: Per-Prompt Hook (UserPromptSubmit)
   131→
   132→`scripts/graph_context_hook.py` fires on every user prompt. It:
   133→1. Extracts keywords using sliding 3/2/1-word windows
   134→2. Queries SurrealDB for the best-matching neuron
   135→3. Injects ~275 tokens of context (neuron + 5 outbound + 5 inbound connections)
   136→4. Is silent (zero output) when no match or SurrealDB unavailable
   137→
   138→Registered in `.claude/settings.json`:
   139→```json
   140→{
   141→  "type": "command",
   142→  "command": "python3 /home/mike-anderson/vaults/cohezion-vault/scripts/graph_context_hook.py"
   143→}
   144→```
   145→
   146→**Hook output format:**
   147→```
   148→--- Graph Context: "Pilot Wave Theory" ---
   149→[████░] 0.85 Pilot Wave Theory (mature, cortex, out:8 in:12)
   150→  Path: cortex/pilot-wave-theory.md
   151→  Tags: quantum, bohmian, determinism
   152→  -> [████░] 0.82 Quantum Potential (cortex)
   153→  <- [███░░] 0.71 David Bohm (sensory)
   154→────────────────────────────────────────
   155→```
   156→
   157→**Latency:** ~259ms total (well within 300ms budget). Skip patterns prevent firing
   158→on short replies ("yes", "no", "/commands", numbers).
   159→
   160→## Reading the Output
   161→
   162→Each neuron line shows:
   163→
   164→```
   165→[████░] 0.85 Quantum Entanglement (mature, cortex, out:12 in:8)
   166→ ^^^^^  ^^^^  ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^    ^^^^^^  ^^^^^^  ^^^^^^^^^^^
   167→ bar    act   title                stage   cluster synapses
   168→```
   169→
   170→- **Activation bar**: Visual 5-block indicator (0.0 = [░░░░░], 1.0 = [█████])
   171→- **Activation**: 0.0-1.0 score based on content, links, and recency
   172→- **Stage**: embryo → growing → mature → resting
   173→- **Cluster**: Which vault directory/domain
   174→- **Synapses**: outbound and inbound link counts
   175→
   176→## Workflow Pattern
   177→
   178→1. **Start with search** — find the neuron you care about
   179→2. **Run neighborhood** — see its immediate connections
   180→3. **Use hops for broader context** — 2-hop shows the extended network
   181→4. **Read only the files that matter** — use the graph to decide WHICH files to read
   182→
   183→This inverts the typical workflow: instead of reading files to discover relationships,
   184→you discover relationships first and read files only when needed.
   185→
   186→## Requirements
   187→
   188→- SurrealDB running on port 8001 (check: `ss -tlnp | grep 8001`)
   189→- Context functions installed (run: `curl -X POST http://localhost:8001/sql -u root:root -H "surreal-ns: cohezion" -H "surreal-db: vault" --data-binary @scripts/dba/context_functions.surql`)
   190→- Python 3.10+ (uses stdlib only — no pip dependencies)
   191→

```
     1→---
     2→name: graph-reactor
     3→description: |
     4→  Pre-compute SurrealDB graph intelligence into vault markdown files so Claude
     5→  reads one file at session start instead of making runtime curl calls.
     6→  Use when: (1) building a file-watching daemon that also needs to surface graph
     7→  health, (2) optimizing Claude token costs for SurrealDB-backed vaults,
     8→  (3) implementing "ambient intelligence" — always-fresh computed state written
     9→  at change-time so read-time is zero-cost, (4) you want a throttled, deduped
    10→  reactor that fires inside an existing inotify loop.
    11→  Key insight: pre-computing 5 SurrealDB queries (~5,000 tokens if called at
    12→  session start) into a single pre-written markdown file costs ~500 tokens to
    13→  read — 10x token compression, zero query latency, zero runtime db calls.
    14→author: Claude Code
    15→version: 1.0.0
    16→---
    17→
    18→# GraphReactor — Pre-Computed Graph Intelligence
    19→
    20→## Problem
    21→
    22→Claude needs graph awareness (vault health, dark countries, orphan neurons, etc.)
    23→at the start of every session. Naively, this means 5+ SurrealDB curl calls during
    24→session init, costing ~5,000 tokens and adding latency.
    25→
    26→**Better:** A daemon pre-computes the results whenever the vault changes and writes
    27→a single markdown file. Claude reads the file at session start — one file read,
    28→zero db queries, ~500 tokens.
    29→
    30→## Key Insight: Not All Tokens Are Created Equal
    31→
    32→- 5 SurrealDB runtime queries → ~5,000 tokens (expensive, every session)
    33→- 1 pre-written markdown file → ~500 tokens (cheap, always fresh)
    34→- Daemon runs queries when the user is already editing — zero extra latency
    35→
    36→This is **compound engineering**: the reactor fires automatically inside the
    37→existing inotify watch loop. No new process, no new scheduler.
    38→
    39→## Architecture
    40→
    41→```
    42→inotify event → watch_vault() event loop
    43→                        ↓ (every 60s, if events occurred)
    44→                  GraphReactor.maybe_react()
    45→                        ↓
    46→                  5 SurrealDB queries (parallel conceptually, sequential in code)
    47→                        ↓
    48→                  build markdown string
    49→                        ↓ (only if content changed — SHA-256 hash check)
    50→                  write metabolism/graph-alerts.md
    51→```
    52→
    53→## Complete Implementation
    54→
    55→```python
    56→import hashlib
    57→import time
    58→from pathlib import Path
    59→
    60→REACTOR_THROTTLE_SECS = 60
    61→ALERTS_FILE = Path("metabolism/graph-alerts.md")
    62→
    63→class GraphReactor:
    64→    """Pre-computes graph intelligence into vault files for Claude to read."""
    65→
    66→    def __init__(self, db):
    67→        self.db = db
    68→        self._last_run = 0.0
    69→        self._last_hash = ""
    70→
    71→    def maybe_react(self, changed_country: str = "") -> bool:
    72→        """Run reactor if throttle has elapsed. Returns True if file was written."""
    73→        now = time.time()
    74→        if now - self._last_run < REACTOR_THROTTLE_SECS:
    75→            return False
    76→        self._last_run = now
    77→        return self._run(changed_country)
    78→
    79→    def _run(self, changed_country: str = "") -> bool:
    80→        try:
    81→            dark = self._query_dark_countries()
    82→            low = self._query_low_countries()
    83→            orphans = self._query_orphan_neurons()
    84→            resting = self._query_resting_notes()
    85→            hiho = self._query_recent_hiho()
    86→            gaps = self._query_synapse_gaps()
    87→        except Exception as e:
    88→            print(f"GraphReactor query error: {e}", file=sys.stderr)
    89→            return False
    90→
    91→        content = self._build_markdown(dark, low, orphans, resting, hiho, gaps)
    92→        content_hash = hashlib.sha256(content.encode()).hexdigest()
    93→
    94→        if content_hash == self._last_hash:
    95→            return False  # No change — don't write
    96→
    97→        self._last_hash = content_hash
    98→        ALERTS_FILE.parent.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    99→        ALERTS_FILE.write_text(content, encoding="utf-8")
   100→        return True
   101→
   102→    def _build_markdown(self, dark, low, orphans, resting, hiho, gaps) -> str:
   103→        from datetime import date
   104→        today = date.today().isoformat()
   105→        lines = [
   106→            "---",
   107→            "title: Graph Alerts — Live Dashboard",
   108→            f"date: {today}",
   109→            "tags: [metabolism, graph-reactor, auto-generated]",
   110→            "aspect: connective",
   111→            "---",
   112→            "",
   113→            "# Graph Alerts",
   114→            "",
   115→            f"> Auto-generated by `vault_sync.py` GraphReactor on {today}.",
   116→            "> Read by vault-keeper at session start. Do not edit manually.",
   117→            "",
   118→        ]
   119→        # Dark countries
   120→        lines += ["## Dark Countries (health < 0.3)", ""]
   121→        if dark:
   122→            lines += ["| Country | Health | Neurons | Action |",
   123→                      "|---------|--------|---------|--------|"]
   124→            for r in dark:
   125→                lines.append(
   126→                    f"| {r['name']} | {r['health']:.3f} | {r['neuron_count']} "
   127→                    f"| Flesh out notes in `{r['name']}/` |"
   128→                )
   129→        else:
   130→            lines.append("None. All countries above dark threshold.")
   131→        lines.append("")
   132→        # ... (build remaining sections similarly)
   133→        return "\n".join(lines)
   134→```
   135→
   136→## Query Patterns
   137→
   138→### Dark Countries (health < 0.3)
   139→
   140→```python
   141→def _query_dark_countries(self) -> list[dict]:
   142→    results = self.db.query(
   143→        "SELECT name, health, neuron_count FROM country "
   144→        "WHERE health < 0.3 ORDER BY health ASC LIMIT 10;"
   145→    )
   146→    return results or []
   147→```
   148→
   149→### Orphan Neurons (high activation, 0 inbound)
   150→
   151→```python
   152→def _query_orphan_neurons(self) -> list[dict]:
   153→    results = self.db.query(
   154→        "SELECT path, activation, synapse_out FROM neuron "
   155→        "WHERE synapse_in = 0 AND activation > 0.7 "
   156→        "ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 10;"
   157→    )
   158→    return results or []
   159→```
   160→
   161→### Synapse Gaps (tag-based co-occurrence without synapses)
   162→
   163→Two neurons that share 2+ tags but have no synapse are missed connections:
   164→
   165→```python
   166→def _query_synapse_gaps(self) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
   167→    """Find cortex neuron pairs sharing 2+ tags but no synapse."""
   168→    neurons = self.db.query(
   169→        "SELECT id, path, tags FROM neuron "
   170→        "WHERE cluster_id = 'cortex' AND array::len(tags) > 0 "
   171→        "ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 100;"
   172→    )
   173→    if not neurons:
   174→        return []
   175→
   176→    # Build tag → neuron index
   177→    tag_to_neurons: dict[str, list] = {}
   178→    for n in neurons:
   179→        for tag in (n.get("tags") or []):
   180→            tag_to_neurons.setdefault(tag, []).append(n)
   181→
   182→    # Find pairs sharing 2+ tags
   183→    from itertools import combinations
   184→    pair_shared: dict[tuple, set] = {}
   185→    for tag, ns in tag_to_neurons.items():
   186→        for a, b in combinations(ns, 2):
   187→            key = (min(str(a["id"]), str(b["id"])),
   188→                   max(str(a["id"]), str(b["id"])))
   189→            pair_shared.setdefault(key, set()).add(tag)
   190→
   191→    # Filter to pairs with 2+ shared tags
   192→    candidates = [(k, v) for k, v in pair_shared.items() if len(v) >= 2]
   193→    candidates.sort(key=lambda x: -len(x[1]))
   194→
   195→    # Check which pairs already have synapses
   196→    gaps = []
   197→    for (id_a, id_b), _ in candidates[:30]:
   198→        exists = self.db.query(
   199→            f"SELECT count() FROM synapse "
   200→            f"WHERE (in = {id_a} AND out = {id_b}) "
   201→            f"OR (in = {id_b} AND out = {id_a}) GROUP ALL;"
   202→        )
   203→        count = exists[0].get("count", 0) if exists else 0
   204→        if count == 0:
   205→            # Resolve to note names for display
   206→            n_a = next((n for n in neurons if str(n["id"]) == id_a), None)
   207→            n_b = next((n for n in neurons if str(n["id"]) == id_b), None)
   208→            if n_a and n_b:
   209→                name_a = Path(n_a["path"]).stem.replace("-", " ").title()
   210→                name_b = Path(n_b["path"]).stem.replace("-", " ").title()
   211→                gaps.append((name_a, name_b))
   212→        if len(gaps) >= 15:
   213→            break
   214→
   215→    return gaps
   216→```
   217→
   218→## Wiring into inotify watch loop
   219→
   220→```python
   221→def watch_vault(db, quiet=False):
   222→    reactor = GraphReactor(db)
   223→    watcher = InotifyWatcher()
   224→    watcher.add_tree(str(VAULT_ROOT), skip_dirs={".git", ".obsidian"})
   225→
   226→    initial_sync()
   227→
   228→    # Force first reactor run immediately after sync
   229→    reactor._last_run = 0
   230→    if reactor.maybe_react():
   231→        if not quiet:
   232→            print("Graph reactor: initial alerts written", file=sys.stderr)
   233→
   234→    pending = {}
   235→    while True:
   236→        events = watcher.read_events(timeout_ms=200)
   237→        # ... process events into pending dict ...
   238→
   239→        # Flush debounced events
   240→        now = time.monotonic()
   241→        for path, (event_type, ts) in list(pending.items()):
   242→            if now - ts >= 0.5:
   243→                del pending[path]
   244→                handle_event(event_type, path, db)
   245→
   246→        # Reactor fires after any events — throttled to 60s
   247→        if events:
   248→            if reactor.maybe_react():
   249→                if not quiet:
   250→                    print("Graph reactor: alerts updated", file=sys.stderr)
   251→```
   252→
   253→## Standalone CLI mode
   254→
   255→For manual regeneration (e.g., from vault-keeper skill):
   256→
   257→```python
   258→elif "--react" in sys.argv:
   259→    reactor = GraphReactor(db)
   260→    reactor._last_run = 0  # Bypass throttle for manual run
   261→    if reactor.maybe_react():
   262→        print(f"Alerts written to {ALERTS_FILE}", file=sys.stderr)
   263→    else:
   264→        print("No changes detected", file=sys.stderr)
   265→```
   266→
   267→```bash
   268→# Manual run
   269→python3 scripts/vault_sync.py --react
   270→
   271→# Daemon mode (starts watcher with reactor inline)
   272→python3 scripts/vault_sync.py --watch &
   273→```
   274→
   275→## vault-keeper Integration
   276→
   277→Update vault-keeper's "Neural Intelligence" section to read the pre-computed file
   278→instead of making runtime curl calls:
   279→
   280→```markdown
   281→At session start, read `metabolism/graph-alerts.md` for pre-computed graph health.
   282→If it's stale or missing: `python3 scripts/vault_sync.py --react`
   283→```
   284→
   285→This saves ~5,000 tokens per Claude session that would otherwise go to SurrealDB
   286→curl calls.
   287→
   288→## Throttle Tuning
   289→
   290→| Activity Level | Recommended Throttle |
   291→|---------------|---------------------|
   292→| Active editing (many changes/min) | 60s (default) |
   293→| Occasional changes | 30s |
   294→| Bulk imports | 120s (avoid thrashing) |
   295→| Never write unnecessarily | Use content-hash dedup (always) |
   296→
   297→## Pitfalls
   298→
   299→| Pitfall | Fix |
   300→|---------|-----|
   301→| Writes file on every event | Add content-hash dedup (SHA-256) |
   302→| Queries fail silently | Wrap `_run()` in try/except, return False |
   303→| Reactor blocks event loop | All queries are synchronous but fast (<200ms total) |
   304→| First run misses initial state | Force `_last_run = 0` after initial sync |
   305→| SurrealDB field-projection HTTP 400 | Use `SELECT *` and filter in Python (see surrealdb-debug skill) |
   306→

-- ============================================================================
-- TRIUNE VAULT SCHEMA — SurrealDB 3.0
-- The Akashic Foundation: Neural, Aboriginal, Physics, and Theosophical layers
-- ============================================================================
-- Created: 2026-03-09
-- Purpose: Define the complete Triune Vault connectome
--
-- Tables:
--   neuron         — every vault note
--   synapse        — every wiki-link (explicit) + latent/dream connections
--   country        — domain clusters (Aboriginal layer)
--   songline       — narrative knowledge paths across Countries
--   kinship        — deeper-than-synapse relationships
--   dream          — daily cross-domain resonance events
--   hiho_event     — coherence fusion events (Physics layer)
--   neuron_history — Akashic Records (Theosophical layer)
-- ============================================================================

-- =========================
-- TABLE: neuron
-- Every vault note is a neuron
-- =========================
DEFINE TABLE neuron SCHEMAFULL;

DEFINE FIELD path ON TABLE neuron TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD title ON TABLE neuron TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD aspect ON TABLE neuron TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN ['knower', 'thinker', 'doer', 'connective'];
DEFINE FIELD activation ON TABLE neuron TYPE float DEFAULT 0.5;
DEFINE FIELD stage ON TABLE neuron TYPE string
  ASSERT $value IN ['embryo', 'growing', 'mature', 'resting', 'composting', 'renewed'];
DEFINE FIELD last_fired ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE FIELD cluster_id ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD synapse_out ON TABLE neuron TYPE int DEFAULT 0;
DEFINE FIELD synapse_in ON TABLE neuron TYPE int DEFAULT 0;
DEFINE FIELD word_count ON TABLE neuron TYPE int DEFAULT 0;
DEFINE FIELD tags ON TABLE neuron TYPE array DEFAULT [];
DEFINE FIELD embedding ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<array>;
DEFINE FIELD created ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE FIELD modified ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<datetime>;
DEFINE FIELD directory ON TABLE neuron TYPE option<string>;

-- Indexes for common queries
DEFINE INDEX idx_neuron_path ON TABLE neuron COLUMNS path UNIQUE;
DEFINE INDEX idx_neuron_aspect ON TABLE neuron COLUMNS aspect;
DEFINE INDEX idx_neuron_stage ON TABLE neuron COLUMNS stage;
DEFINE INDEX idx_neuron_cluster ON TABLE neuron COLUMNS cluster_id;
DEFINE INDEX idx_neuron_activation ON TABLE neuron COLUMNS activation;

-- =========================
-- TABLE: synapse
-- Every wiki-link is a synapse (edge table)
-- =========================
DEFINE TABLE synapse SCHEMAFULL TYPE RELATION IN neuron OUT neuron;

DEFINE FIELD weight ON TABLE synapse TYPE float DEFAULT 1.0;
DEFINE FIELD link_type ON TABLE synapse TYPE string DEFAULT 'explicit'
  ASSERT $value IN ['explicit', 'latent', 'dream', 'songline'];
DEFINE FIELD created ON TABLE synapse TYPE option<datetime>;

-- =========================
-- TABLE: country
-- Domain clusters (Aboriginal layer)
-- =========================
DEFINE TABLE country SCHEMAFULL;

DEFINE FIELD name ON TABLE country TYPE string;
DEFINE FIELD description ON TABLE country TYPE option<string>;
DEFINE FIELD elders ON TABLE country TYPE array DEFAULT [];
DEFINE FIELD health ON TABLE country TYPE float DEFAULT 0.5;
DEFINE FIELD centroid ON TABLE country TYPE option<array>;
DEFINE FIELD neuron_count ON TABLE country TYPE int DEFAULT 0;

DEFINE INDEX idx_country_name ON TABLE country COLUMNS name UNIQUE;

-- =========================
-- TABLE: country_kin
-- Kinship between Countries
-- =========================
DEFINE TABLE country_kin SCHEMAFULL TYPE RELATION IN country OUT country;
```

-- Context Query Functions for Cohezion Vault Graph
-- These functions return token-efficient context for agent consumption.
-- Install: surreal import --conn http://localhost:8001 --ns cohezion --db vault context_functions.surql

-- ─── fn::context_neighborhood ────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Returns the immediate neighborhood of a neuron: its metadata, outbound
-- targets, inbound sources, and cluster siblings. One call, full local context.
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::context_neighborhood(neuron:cortex_sarfatti_post_quantum_mechanics_and_toe_md);
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::context_neighborhood($nid: record<neuron>) {
    LET $n = (SELECT title, activation, stage, cluster_id, aspect, tags,
                     synapse_out, synapse_in, word_count, path
              FROM ONLY $nid);

    LET $outbound = (SELECT out.id AS id, out.title AS title,
                            out.activation AS activation, out.stage AS stage,
                            out.cluster_id AS cluster
                     FROM synapse WHERE in = $nid);

    LET $inbound  = (SELECT in.id AS id, in.title AS title,
                            in.activation AS activation, in.stage AS stage,
                            in.cluster_id AS cluster
                     FROM synapse WHERE out = $nid);

    LET $cluster_top = (SELECT id, title, activation, stage
                        FROM neuron
                        WHERE cluster_id = $n.cluster_id AND id != $nid
                        ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 5);

    RETURN {
        neuron: $n,
        outbound: $outbound,
        inbound: $inbound,
        cluster_top: $cluster_top
    };
};

-- ─── fn::context_cluster ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Returns a cluster summary: top neurons by activation, total count, average
-- activation, coherence score. Useful for "what's in this domain?"
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::context_cluster("cortex");
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::context_cluster($cluster_name: string) {
    LET $neurons = (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, synapse_out, synapse_in
                    FROM neuron
                    WHERE cluster_id = $cluster_name
                    ORDER BY activation DESC);

    LET $total = array::len($neurons);
    LET $avg_act = math::mean($neurons.activation);
    LET $coherence = fn::compute_coherence($cluster_name);

    LET $top = (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, synapse_out, synapse_in
                FROM neuron
                WHERE cluster_id = $cluster_name
                ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 10);

    RETURN {
        cluster: $cluster_name,
        total_neurons: $total,
        avg_activation: $avg_act,
        coherence: $coherence,
        top_neurons: $top
    };
};

-- ─── fn::context_search ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Finds neurons by title substring match, returns compact results sorted by
-- activation. Use for "find everything related to X" queries.
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::context_search("quantum");
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::context_search($query: string) {
    LET $matches = (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, cluster_id, aspect,
                           synapse_out, synapse_in, path
                    FROM neuron
                    WHERE string::contains(string::lowercase(title), string::lowercase($query))
                       OR string::contains(string::lowercase(<string> tags), string::lowercase($query))
                    ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 20);

    RETURN $matches;
};

-- ─── fn::context_hops ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Returns all neurons reachable within N hops from a starting neuron.
-- Hop 1 = direct neighbors, Hop 2 = neighbors of neighbors.
-- Returns deduplicated results sorted by activation.
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::context_hops(neuron:cortex_sarfatti_post_quantum_mechanics_and_toe_md, 2);
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::context_hops($nid: record<neuron>, $depth: int) {
    -- Hop 1: direct connections (both directions)
    LET $hop1_ids = array::union(
        (SELECT VALUE out FROM synapse WHERE in = $nid),
        (SELECT VALUE in FROM synapse WHERE out = $nid)
    );

    IF $depth <= 1 {
        RETURN (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, cluster_id, synapse_out, synapse_in
                FROM neuron WHERE id IN $hop1_ids
                ORDER BY activation DESC);
    };

    -- Hop 2: connections of hop1 neurons
    LET $hop2_ids = array::union(
        (SELECT VALUE out FROM synapse WHERE in IN $hop1_ids AND out != $nid AND out NOT IN $hop1_ids),
        (SELECT VALUE in FROM synapse WHERE out IN $hop1_ids AND in != $nid AND in NOT IN $hop1_ids)
    );

    LET $all_ids = array::union($hop1_ids, $hop2_ids);

    RETURN (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, cluster_id, synapse_out, synapse_in
            FROM neuron WHERE id IN $all_ids
            ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 50);
};

-- ─── fn::context_bridges ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Finds neurons that bridge two clusters — nodes with synapses into both.
-- These are the cross-domain connectors, the most token-valuable context.
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::context_bridges("cortex", "sensory");
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::context_bridges($cluster_a: string, $cluster_b: string) {
    -- Neurons in cluster_a that link to cluster_b
    LET $a_to_b = (SELECT VALUE in FROM synapse
                   WHERE in.cluster_id = $cluster_a AND out.cluster_id = $cluster_b);

    -- Neurons in cluster_b that link to cluster_a
    LET $b_to_a = (SELECT VALUE in FROM synapse
                   WHERE in.cluster_id = $cluster_b AND out.cluster_id = $cluster_a);

    LET $bridge_ids = array::union($a_to_b, $b_to_a);

    RETURN (SELECT id, title, activation, stage, cluster_id, synapse_out, synapse_in, path
            FROM neuron WHERE id IN $bridge_ids
            ORDER BY activation DESC LIMIT 20);
};

-- ─── fn::vault_stats ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
-- Global vault health snapshot. One call for dashboard-level awareness.
--
-- Usage: SELECT * FROM fn::vault_stats();
DEFINE FUNCTION fn::vault_stats() {
    LET $total = array::len((SELECT id FROM neuron));
    LET $synapses = array::len((SELECT id FROM synapse));
    LET $clusters = (SELECT cluster_id, count() AS n, math::mean(activation) AS avg_act
                     FROM neuron GROUP BY cluster_id ORDER BY n DESC);
    LET $stages = (SELECT stage, count() AS n FROM neuron GROUP BY stage);

    RETURN {
        total_neurons: $total[0].n,
        total_synapses: $synapses[0].n,
        clusters: $clusters,
        stage_distribution: $stages
    };
};
```

"""Agent context operations for Phase 1 SurrealDB integration.

Tracks agent sessions, decisions, and outcomes to enable research lineage queries.
"""

import json
import logging
import uuid
from datetime import UTC, datetime
from typing import Any


logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)


class AgentContextOps:
    """Operations for tracking agent sessions, decisions, and outcomes in SurrealDB."""

    def __init__(self, surrealdb_sync):
        """Initialize with SurrealDBSync instance.

        Args:
            surrealdb_sync: SurrealDBSync instance for database operations
        """
        self.db = surrealdb_sync

    def track_session(
        self,
        agent_id: str,
        goals: list[str],
        model_used: str = "claude-haiku-4-5",
        phase: str = "research",
    ) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """Create a new agent session node.

        Args:
            agent_id: Unique agent identifier (e.g., 'integration-engineer')
            goals: List of goals for the session
            model_used: Model name (default: claude-haiku-4-5)
            phase: Session phase (research, decision, implementation, validation)

        Returns:
            Dict with session_id, status, timestamp
        """
        try:
            session_id = f"agent_session:{uuid.uuid4()!s}"
            now = datetime.now(UTC).isoformat()

            query = f"""
                CREATE agent_session SET
                    agent_id = '{agent_id}',
                    session_id = '{session_id}',
                    start_time = '{now}',
                    end_time = null,
                    model_used = '{model_used}',
                    total_tokens = 0,
                    cost_usd = 0.0,
                    phase = '{phase}',
                    status = 'in_progress',
                    goals = {json.dumps(goals)},
                    outcome_summary = null
                RETURN id, agent_id, session_id, start_time, status
            """

            result = self.db._execute_query(query)
            if result and len(result) > 0:
                record = result[0]
                logger.info(f"Created agent session: {session_id}")
                return {
                    "success": True,
                    "session_id": session_id,
                    "agent_id": agent_id,
                    "status": "in_progress",
                    "timestamp": now,
                }
            else:
                return {
                    "success": False,
                    "error": "Failed to create session in SurrealDB",
                }
```

> AGENT

Good — I have a clear picture of the current system. Here's what I see:

**Current architecture:**
- `neuron/synapse/country` schema in SurrealDB (1578 neurons, ~8800 synapses) with rich metadata
- `graph_context.py` — CLI tool with 8 commands, used by agents via bash
- `context_functions.surql` — SurrealDB stored functions (`fn::context_neighborhood`, `fn::context_cluster`, etc.)
- `graph_context_hook.py` — per-prompt injection (~275 tokens), fires on every user prompt
- `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` — pre-computed session-start briefing (~910 tokens)
- `metabolism/graph-alerts.md` — GraphReactor pre-computed health dashboard
- MCP server (`surrealdb_query` tool) — raw SurrealQL passthrough, ~35 other tools
- `agent_context.py` — agent session/decision tracking (separate from vault graph)

**Pain points I can see:**
The MCP server exposes `surrealdb_query` as a raw SQL passthrough with no graph-aware API surface. Agents have to know SurrealQL to query the graph — there's no "natural language to graph" bridge. The graph-context CLI is only usable from the terminal, not from within MCP tool calls.

First question:

**When you say "easily operable by humans and agents" — what's the primary friction point you're hitting today?** 

- (a) Agents (Claude) can't easily query the graph during tasks — they have to write SurrealQL or run bash commands
- (b) The graph data is incomplete or stale — sync gaps mean queries return wrong/missing data
- (c) Humans can't easily inspect or navigate the graph without running CLI commands
- (d) Something else — a specific workflow that's broken or missing

> DEVELOPER

a,b,c and we need to be looking towards the future.

> AGENT

All three — and you want to build toward something. That shapes this significantly.

Second question:

**When you picture this system working well in 6-12 months, which of these feels closest to the vision?**

- **(a) The vault as a living research brain** — agents autonomously traverse the graph to reason across domains, surface connections, and continuously enrich the knowledge (the graph drives agent cognition, not just stores notes)
- **(b) A queryable second brain for humans** — you can ask "what do I know about X?" or "how does A connect to B?" in natural language and get structured answers drawn from the graph, without touching the CLI
- **(c) Multi-agent coordination substrate** — the graph is the shared memory between multiple specialized agents (researcher, linker, synthesizer), each reading/writing to it as a shared world-model
- **(d) All three — the graph as a unified intelligence layer** that serves agents, humans, and inter-agent coordination from a single consistent interface

> DEVELOPER

> AGENT

Third question:

**The current system has two separate access paths to the graph: the bash CLI (`graph_context.py`) and the MCP server (`surrealdb_query` raw passthrough). For the unified layer, which direction feels right?**

- **(a) Elevate the MCP server** — the graph API lives in `cloud-vault-mcp` as first-class MCP tools (`graph_neighborhood`, `graph_search`, `graph_write`, etc.), accessible to all MCP clients. The bash CLI becomes a thin wrapper around those tools
- **(b) Keep the CLI as the canonical interface** — `graph_context.py` is the source of truth, and the MCP server calls it as a subprocess. Simple, proven, Python-only
- **(c) Build a dedicated Graph API layer** — a clean HTTP service that both the CLI and MCP server call. Agents, humans, and scripts all go through the same endpoints, same query semantics
- **(d) Consolidate everything into SurrealDB functions** — push more intelligence into `context_functions.surql` server-side, then both CLI and MCP call the same stored functions. Single source of truth is the DB itself

Counting votes from across the constituency:

| Voter | (a) MCP-first | (b) CLI canonical | (c) Dedicated API | (d) SurrealDB functions |
|-------|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| **Human at terminal** | △ needs MCP client | ✓ natural | ✓ curlable | ✗ must know SurrealQL |
| **Claude via MCP** | ✓ native tools | △ subprocess call | ✓ httpx call | △ raw passthrough |
| **Multi-agent coord** | ✓ shared interface | △ clunky | ✓ shared substrate | ✓ single truth |
| **Maintainability** | ✗ already 35+ tools | ✓ proven simple | ✗ new service | ✓ DB-native |
| **Resilience** | △ MCP server dependency | ✓ no extra deps | ✗ two failure points | ✓ survives infra chaos |

**Tally: No clear winner — but there's a coalition government.**

(d) wins the "single source of truth" vote. (a) wins the "agent operability" vote. (b) wins "don't break what works." (c) loses — nobody wants a new service to maintain after the 22-hour outage we just fixed.

```
SurrealDB stored functions  ← canonical intelligence layer
        ↑               ↑
  MCP graph tools      CLI commands
  (agents / Claude)   (humans / scripts)
```

Same query logic everywhere. No new service. The MCP tools become thin wrappers over the same `fn::context_*` functions the CLI already calls. Does that coalition work for you?
