> AGENT

Good — iterative and pragmatic. Next question:

**Right now agents can read the graph but can they meaningfully write back to it?** Specifically, should agents be able to:

- **(a) Read-only** — the graph reflects the vault's markdown files (sync daemon owns all writes), agents never write directly to the graph
- **(b) Annotation writes only** — agents can add metadata (activation bumps, tags, stage promotions) but can't create/delete neurons or synapses — structural writes stay with the sync daemon
- **(c) Full read-write** — agents can create neurons, add synapses, evolve the graph autonomously — the graph becomes a living workspace that diverges from the markdown if needed
- **(d) Event-sourced writes** — agents emit structured "graph events" (discoveries, connections, insights) that get queued and applied by the sync daemon, so the vault files always stay canonical but agent intelligence flows in asynchronously

> DEVELOPER

we need agentic democratic consensus for this

> AGENT

Polling the agents who actually live in this system:

| Agent | (a) Read-only | (b) Annotation writes | (c) Full read-write | (d) Event-sourced |
|-------|:---:|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| **Vault Sync Daemon** | ✓ no conflicts | ✓ clean boundary | ✗ who wins conflicts? | ✓ I stay canonical |
| **Claude (research)** | △ can't record discoveries | ✓ enough to be useful | ✓ powerful | ✓ safe async |
| **Dreaming Engine** | ✗ *already writes dream synapses* | △ needs synapse creation | ✓ | ✓ |
| **Vault Keeper** | ✗ needs stage promotion | ✓ metadata = its job | overkill | ✓ |
| **Multi-agent coord** | ✗ agents can't share finds | ✓ each annotates | △ conflict risk | ✓ natural |
| **Data integrity** | ✓ safest | ✓ metadata recomputable | ✗ agent bugs corrupt | ✓ auditable |

**Key observation:** (a) is already eliminated — the dreaming engine *already writes* `link_type: dream` synapses. That boundary is crossed. The schema even encodes it: `link_type IN ['explicit', 'latent', 'dream', 'songline']`.

**Coalition: (b) now, with (d) as the upgrade path.**

The existing schema already defines the boundary perfectly:

```
Sync daemon owns → explicit synapses + neuron CRUD (from markdown)
Agents own      → latent/dream synapses + metadata (activation, stage, tags)
```

(d) is the right long-term architecture but adds a queue and event log — more complexity than this phase needs. Start with (b)'s clean boundary, which the schema already enforces. Upgrade to (d) when multi-agent coordination gets complex enough to need conflict resolution.

   122→
   123→## Design Approach: Dimensional Projection
   124→
   125→### The Challenge
   126→**Problem**: Humans can only visualize 3D space directly
   127→
   128→**Solution**: Dynamic dimensional projection with user control
   129→
   130→### Projection Strategy
   131→
   132→1. **Primary Axes** (X, Y, Z) - Always visible
   133→   - User selects which 3 dimensions map to X/Y/Z
   134→   - Default: Spatial positioning (domain clustering)
   135→
   136→2. **Secondary Encoding** - Visual properties
   137→   - **Dimension 4-6**: Node size, color, opacity
   138→   - **Dimension 7-9**: Edge thickness, color, pattern
   139→   - **Dimension 10-12**: Animation, glow, particle effects
   140→
   141→3. **Interactive Slicing** - Filter/constrain dimensions
   142→   - Slider for each dimension (12 total)
   143→   - Constrain to range (e.g., "show only papers from 2025-2026")
   144→   - Live filter updates
   145→
   146→### Dimensional Mapping Table
   147→
   148→| Dimension | Default Axis | Visual Encoding | Filter Control |
   149→|-----------|-------------|-----------------|----------------|
   150→| 1. X Position | Domain cluster | X-axis position | Domain selector |
   151→| 2. Y Position | Connectivity | Y-axis position | Min/max links |
   152→| 3. Z Position | Temporal | Z-axis position | Date range |
   153→| 4. Domain Affinity | Color | Node color hue | Domain filter |
   154→| 5. Depth Level | Size | Node radius | Theory ↔ Applied |
   155→| 6. Citation Impact | Glow | Node glow intensity | Min citations |
   156→| 7. Recency | Opacity | Node alpha | Age filter |
   157→| 8. Cross-Domain | Edge thickness | Connection width | Bridge score |
   158→| 9. User Interest | Animation | Pulse rate | Interaction count |
   159→| 10. Semantic Sim | Edge color | Connection hue | Similarity threshold |
   160→| 11. Completion | Border | Node outline style | Enrichment % |
   161→| 12. Agent Affinity | Particles | Node particle count | Relevance score |

   194→### Phase 2: Dimensional Computation (Week 2-3)
   195→
   196→**Objective**: Calculate all 12 dimensions for each node/edge
   197→
   198→#### 2.1: Static Dimensions (Computed Once)
   199→```python
   200→# Dimension computations in Cloud Vault MCP
   201→
   202→def compute_temporal_dimension(paper):
   203→    """Dimension 3: Temporal - Publication date normalized"""
   204→    pub_date = paper.frontmatter.get('date')
   205→    return normalize_date_to_axis(pub_date)
   206→
   207→def compute_connectivity_dimension(paper):
   208→    """Dimension 6: Connectivity Density - Link count"""
   209→    links = count_wiki_links(paper.content)
   210→    return normalize(links, min=0, max=100)
   211→
   212→def compute_domain_dimension(paper):
   213→    """Dimension 1/4: Domain affinity - Multi-hot encoding"""
   214→    tags = paper.frontmatter.get('tags', [])
   215→    return map_tags_to_domain_vector(tags)
   216→
   217→def compute_conceptual_depth(paper):
   218→    """Dimension 5: Theory ↔ Application"""
   219→    # NLP analysis of abstract/summary
   220→    theory_keywords = ['theoretical', 'framework', 'model', 'principle']
   221→    applied_keywords = ['implementation', 'application', 'results', 'experiment']
   222→    return calculate_theory_vs_applied_score(paper)
   223→```
   224→
   225→#### 2.2: Dynamic Dimensions (Computed on Query)
   226→```python
   227→def compute_semantic_similarity(paper1, paper2):
   228→    """Dimension 10: NLP-based content similarity"""
   229→    embedding1 = get_paper_embedding(paper1)  # via OpenAI/Anthropic
   230→    embedding2 = get_paper_embedding(paper2)
   231→    return cosine_similarity(embedding1, embedding2)
   232→
   233→def compute_agent_affinity(paper, agent_context):
   234→    """Dimension 12: Relevance to current agent journey"""
   235→    current_goals = agent_context.get('goals')
   236→    current_concepts = agent_context.get('active_concepts')
   237→    return calculate_relevance_score(paper, current_goals, current_concepts)
   238→```

   242→- [ ] Record bookmarks/favorites
   243→- [ ] Log time-on-page for papers
   244→- [ ] Update Dimension 9 (User Interest) based on interactions
   245→
   246→**Deliverable**: All nodes have 12-dimensional coordinates in SurrealDB

   290→
   291→#### 3.3: Key Components
   292→
   293→**Graph Renderer** (Three.js/WebGL):
   294→```typescript
   295→// src/viz/GraphRenderer.tsx
   296→import { Canvas } from '@react-three/fiber';
   297→import { OrbitControls, Stats } from '@react-three/drei';
   298→
   299→export const GraphRenderer = ({ nodes, edges, projection }) => {
   300→  return (
   301→    <Canvas camera={{ position: [0, 0, 100], fov: 75 }}>
   302→      <ambientLight intensity={0.5} />
   303→      <pointLight position={[10, 10, 10]} />
   304→
   305→      {/* Nodes */}
   306→      {nodes.map(node => (
   307→        <NodeRenderer
   308→          key={node.id}
   309→          position={projection.project(node.dimensions)}
   310→          dimensions={node.dimensions}
   311→          onClick={() => openFile(node.file)}
   312→        />
   313→      ))}
   314→
   315→      {/* Edges */}
   316→      {edges.map(edge => (
   317→        <EdgeRenderer
   318→          key={edge.id}
   319→          start={projection.project(edge.source.dimensions)}
   320→          end={projection.project(edge.target.dimensions)}
   321→          thickness={edge.dimensions[7]}  // Cross-domain bridging
   322→        />
   323→      ))}
   324→
   325→      <OrbitControls />
   326→      <Stats />
   327→    </Canvas>
   328→  );
   329→};
   330→```
   331→
   332→**Projection Engine**:
   333→```typescript
   334→// src/viz/projectionEngine.ts
   335→export class ProjectionEngine {
   336→  constructor(
   337→    private axisMapping: { x: number; y: number; z: number }
   338→  ) {}
   339→
   340→  project(dimensions: number[]): [number, number, number] {
   341→    // Map selected dimensions to 3D coordinates
   342→    const x = dimensions[this.axisMapping.x] * 100;  // Scale factor
   343→    const y = dimensions[this.axisMapping.y] * 100;
   344→    const z = dimensions[this.axisMapping.z] * 100;
   345→
   346→    return [x, y, z];
   347→  }
   348→
   349→  setAxisMapping(axis: 'x' | 'y' | 'z', dimension: number) {
   350→    this.axisMapping[axis] = dimension;
   351→    // Trigger re-projection
   352→  }
   353→}
   354→```
   355→
   356→**SurrealDB Live Query**:
   357→```typescript
   358→// src/db/surrealClient.ts
   359→import Surreal from 'surrealdb.js';
   360→
   361→export class GraphDatabase {
   362→  private db: Surreal;
   363→
   364→  async connect() {
   365→    this.db = new Surreal('http://localhost:8000/rpc');
   366→    await this.db.signin({ user: 'root', pass: 'root' });
   367→    await this.db.use('cohezion', 'vault');
   368→  }
   369→
   370→  async subscribeToGraph(callback: (data) => void) {
   371→    // Live query for real-time updates
   372→    await this.db.live('SELECT * FROM paper, concept, RELATES', (action, result) => {
   373→      callback({ action, data: result });
   374→    });
   375→  }
   376→
   377→  async queryGraph(filters: DimensionFilters) {
   378→    // Query with dimensional constraints
   379→    const query = `
   380→      SELECT *,
   381→        ->links->concept AS concepts,
   382→        <-links<-paper AS related_papers
   383→      FROM paper
   384→      WHERE domain CONTAINS $domain
   385→        AND temporal BETWEEN $start AND $end
   386→        AND connectivity >= $min_links
   387→    `;
   388→
   389→    return await this.db.query(query, filters);
   390→  }
   391→}
   392→```
   393→
   394→**Deliverable**: Working Obsidian plugin with basic 12D graph visualization

   399→
   400→#### 4.1: Intelligent Recommendations
   401→- [ ] "Papers you might like" based on current view
   402→- [ ] "Explore this cluster" suggestions
   403→- [ ] "Bridge concepts" - find connecting papers
   404→
   405→#### 4.2: Agent Journey Integration
   406→- [ ] Track current agent goals in SurrealDB
   407→- [ ] Highlight relevant papers (Dimension 12)
   408→- [ ] Show "agent path" through graph over time
   409→- [ ] Goal-driven filtering
   410→
   411→#### 4.3: Collaborative Features
   412→- [ ] Multi-user annotations
   413→- [ ] Shared dimensional views (bookmark 12D configurations)
   414→- [ ] Team knowledge maps
   415→
   416→#### 4.4: Export & Analysis
   417→- [ ] Export graph as JSON/GraphML
   418→- [ ] Generate metrics reports
   419→- [ ] Network analysis (centrality, communities)
   420→
   421→**Deliverable**: Production-ready plugin with advanced capabilities
   422→
   423→---
   424→
   425→## Technical Specifications
   426→
   427→### SurrealDB Schema
   428→
   429→```sql
   430→-- Define tables
   431→DEFINE TABLE paper SCHEMAFULL;
   432→DEFINE TABLE concept SCHEMAFULL;
   433→DEFINE TABLE domain SCHEMAFULL;
   434→
   435→-- Paper fields (12 dimensions + metadata)
   436→DEFINE FIELD title ON paper TYPE string;
   437→DEFINE FIELD file_path ON paper TYPE string;
   438→DEFINE FIELD content ON paper TYPE string;
   439→DEFINE FIELD tags ON paper TYPE array;
   440→DEFINE FIELD date ON paper TYPE datetime;
   441→
   442→-- Dimensional fields
   443→DEFINE FIELD dim_spatial_x ON paper TYPE float;
   444→DEFINE FIELD dim_spatial_y ON paper TYPE float;
   445→DEFINE FIELD dim_spatial_z ON paper TYPE float;
   446→DEFINE FIELD dim_temporal ON paper TYPE float;
   447→DEFINE FIELD dim_domain ON paper TYPE array<string>;
   448→DEFINE FIELD dim_connectivity ON paper TYPE int;
   449→DEFINE FIELD dim_depth ON paper TYPE float;
   450→DEFINE FIELD dim_citations ON paper TYPE int;
   451→DEFINE FIELD dim_recency ON paper TYPE float;
   452→DEFINE FIELD dim_bridging ON paper TYPE float;
   453→DEFINE FIELD dim_interest ON paper TYPE float;
   454→DEFINE FIELD dim_similarity ON paper TYPE object;  -- Sparse similarity matrix
   455→DEFINE FIELD dim_completion ON paper TYPE float;
   456→DEFINE FIELD dim_agent_affinity ON paper TYPE object;  -- Context-dependent
   457→
   458→-- Relationships
   459→DEFINE TABLE links TYPE RELATION FROM paper TO concept;
   460→DEFINE TABLE cites TYPE RELATION FROM paper TO paper;
   461→DEFINE TABLE belongs_to TYPE RELATION FROM paper TO domain;
   462→DEFINE TABLE similar_to TYPE RELATION FROM paper TO paper;
   463→
   464→-- Indexes
   465→DEFINE INDEX idx_paper_date ON paper FIELDS date;
   466→DEFINE INDEX idx_paper_tags ON paper FIELDS tags;
   467→DEFINE INDEX idx_paper_connectivity ON paper FIELDS dim_connectivity;
   468→```

   527→
   528→### Agent Journey Tracking
   529→
   530→```python
   531→# In agent code
   532→def update_agent_context(agent_id: str, goals: list, concepts: list):
   533→    """Update agent journey in SurrealDB for dimension 12 calculation."""
   534→    surreal.query("""
   535→        UPDATE agent:$agent_id SET
   536→            current_goals = $goals,
   537→            active_concepts = $concepts,
   538→            timestamp = time::now()
   539→    """, {'agent_id': agent_id, 'goals': goals, 'concepts': concepts})
   540→```

   543→
   544→## Rollout Strategy
   545→
   546→### Milestones
   547→
   548→| Phase | Duration | Deliverable | Success Metric |
   549→|-------|----------|-------------|----------------|
   550→| **Phase 1** | 2 weeks | SurrealDB sync | All vault data in DB, live sync working |
   551→| **Phase 2** | 1 week | 12D coordinates | All nodes have dimensional values |
   552→| **Phase 3** | 2 weeks | Basic plugin | Can visualize graph in 3D with dimension controls |
   553→| **Phase 4** | 1 week | Advanced features | Recommendations, agent integration working |
   554→| **Phase 5** | 1 week | Polish & docs | Production-ready, documented |
   555→
   556→**Total Timeline**: 6-7 weeks to production
   557→
   558→### Risks & Mitigations
   559→
   560→| Risk | Impact | Likelihood | Mitigation |
   561→|------|--------|-----------|-----------|
   562→| SurrealDB performance with large graphs | High | Medium | Index optimization, pagination, clustering |
   563→| 12D projection UX complexity | Medium | High | Clear defaults, guided tours, presets |
   564→| Plugin compatibility issues | Medium | Low | Test with multiple Obsidian versions |
   565→| Real-time sync lag | Low | Medium | Debouncing, background sync, conflict detection |
   566→| Embeddings API cost | Medium | Medium | Cache embeddings, batch requests, use local models |
   567→
   568→---
   569→
   570→## Alternative: Incremental Approach
   571→
   572→If full 12D seems too ambitious initially:
   573→
   574→### Phased Rollout
   575→
   576→**Phase 1-Lite**: 4D Graph (Week 1-2)
   577→- Dimensions: X (domain), Y (connectivity), Z (temporal), Color (completion)
   578→- Simpler to implement
   579→- Proves concept
   580→
   581→**Phase 2-Lite**: 8D Graph (Week 3-4)
   582→- Add: Size (depth), Opacity (recency), Edge thickness (bridging), Glow (citations)
   583→- More informative, still manageable
   584→
   585→**Phase 3-Full**: 12D Graph (Week 5-7)
   586→- Add final 4 dimensions
   587→- Full feature set
   588→
   589→---

    32→
    33→# 12D Graph System - Refined Implementation Plan
    34→
    35→**Status**: Refined after deep analysis of InfraNodus and COHEZION requirements
    36→**Date**: 2026-02-09
    37→**Phase**: Design & Specialist Assembly
    38→
    39→---
    40→
    41→## Executive Summary
    42→
    43→Building a **12-dimensional graph visualization system** that surpasses [InfraNodus](https://infranodus.com/obsidian-plugin) by incorporating:
    44→- 12D analysis vs InfraNodus' 3D (4x richer dimensional space)
    45→- Agent journey tracking (context-aware AI navigation)
    46→- Real-time vault ↔ SurrealDB sync (live updates)
    47→- Compound engineering workflows (multi-agent collaboration)
    48→- Enhanced AI features (gap analysis, betweenness centrality, Claude-powered insights and planning executed with local llms)
    49→
    50→---
    51→
    52→## InfraNodus Feature Analysis
    53→
    54→### Core Features to Abstract & Enhance
    55→
    56→From [InfraNodus documentation](https://infranodus.com/about/how-it-works) and [network analysis](https://infranodus.com/docs/network-analysis):
    57→
    58→#### 1. **Betweenness Centrality**
    59→- **InfraNodus**: Ranks nodes by betweenness centrality (BC) - measures how often a node bridges different clusters
    60→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: Apply BC across 12 dimensions, weight by agent journey affinity
    61→- **Use Case**: Identify "bridge concepts" that connect disparate research domains (e.g., concepts linking quantum computing + AI agents)
    62→
    63→#### 2. **Topical Clustering**
    64→- **InfraNodus**: Uses Louvain community detection algorithm to identify clusters
    65→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: Multi-dimensional clustering using domain tags + semantic similarity + temporal proximity
    66→- **Use Case**: Auto-group papers by research themes, even across traditional domain boundaries
    67→
    68→#### 3. **Force Atlas Layout**
    69→- **InfraNodus**: 2D/3D Force-Atlas layout aligns connected nodes, pushes hubs apart
    70→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: 12D Force-Atlas with projection to 3D viewable space, configurable axis mapping
    71→- **Use Case**: Dynamic re-layout as user explores different dimensional perspectives
    72→
    73→#### 4. **Claude Gap Analysis**
    74→- **InfraNodus**: AI identifies content gaps - concepts that *should* be linked but aren't
    75→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: Opus analyzes 12D topology to suggest missing papers, unexplored research directions -> implemented by local llms
    76→- **Use Case**: "You have 5 papers on quantum computing and 7 on AI agents - explore quantum AI agent architectures?"
    77→
    78→#### 5. **AI Research Questions**
    79→- **InfraNodus**: GPT-4 generates research questions bridging topical clusters
    80→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: Context-aware questions based on current agent task + vault gaps + dimensional position
    81→- **Use Case**: During code review, suggest "How does MCP architecture compare to multi-agent systems?" based on active concepts
    82→
    83→#### 6. **Dynamic Knowledge Graphs**
    84→- **InfraNodus**: Real-time graph updates as notes change
    85→- **COHEZION Enhancement**: Bidirectional sync (vault files ↔ SurrealDB), file watcher, live 3D view updates
    86→- **Use Case**: Add wiki-link to paper → graph updates immediately, new edge appears
    87→
    88→---
    89→
    90→## 12-Dimensional Framework
    91→
    92→### The 12 Dimensions
    93→
    94→| Dimension | Purpose | InfraNodus Equivalent | COHEZION Innovation |
    95→|-----------|---------|----------------------|---------------------|
    96→| **1-3. Spatial (X, Y, Z)** | 3D positioning | Force-Atlas layout | Configurable projection from 12D space |
    97→| **4. Temporal** | Time-based evolution | None | Track knowledge evolution over time |
    98→| **5. Domain Clustering** | Research domain affinity | Topical clusters | Multi-domain bridging detection |
    99→| **6. Connectivity Density** | Number of connections | Betweenness centrality | Weighted by connection strength |
   100→| **7. Conceptual Depth** | Abstraction level | None | Theory ↔ Application spectrum |
   101→| **8. Citation Impact** | Reference frequency | None | Track which papers cite this one |
   102→| **9. Recency/Relevance** | Time-decay weighting | None | Prioritize recent + frequently accessed |
   103→| **10. Cross-Domain Bridging** | Multi-domain papers | Gap analysis | Identify interdisciplinary opportunities |
   104→| **11. User Interest** | Interaction tracking | None | Heat map of user attention |
   105→| **12. Agent Journey Affinity** | Context relevance | None | **COHEZION SIGNATURE FEATURE** |
   106→

   177→
   178→## COHEZION-Optimized Features
   179→
   180→### 1. Agent Journey Mode 🌟 SIGNATURE FEATURE
   181→
   182→**Concept**: Dynamically filter/highlight graph based on current agent context
   183→
   184→**Use Case**:
   185→```
   186→Agent working on: "Implement MCP server for Obsidian vault"
   187→Active concepts: [[mcp-model-context-protocol]], [[agentic-ai]], [[api-design]]
   188→
   189→Graph View transforms:
   190→- Highlights papers with high Agent Journey Affinity (dim_12)
   191→- Fades irrelevant nodes (astrophysics, biology)
   192→- Suggests: "Papers you might need: scaling-agent-systems.md, multi-agent-systems concept"
   193→```
   194→
   195→**Implementation**:
   196→- Track agent's active concepts (from task description + recent messages)
   197→- Compute cosine similarity between agent context embeddings + paper embeddings
   198→- Apply real-time filtering to 3D view
   199→- GPT-4 generates contextual suggestions
   200→
   201→### 2. Compound Engineering Workflows
   202→
   203→**Concept**: Multi-agent collaboration tracking in graph
   204→
   205→**Use Case**:
   206→```
   207→Team of 4 agents working on 12D graph:
   208→- Agent A (SurrealDB specialist) → modifies schema
   209→- Agent B (UI specialist) → designs controls
   210→- Agent C (Math specialist) → projection algorithms
   211→- Agent D (Plugin specialist) → integrates all
   212→
   213→Graph shows:
   214→- Shared concepts highlighted (collaboration points)
   215→- Agent-specific clusters (individual focus areas)
   216→- Handoff edges (where agent B depends on agent A's work)
   217→```
   218→
   219→**Implementation**:
   220→- Add `agent_journey` table tracking active agents + their focus concepts
   221→- Create `agent_collaboration` edges showing dependencies
   222→- Visualize as color-coded node clusters in graph
   223→- Timeline slider showing agent activity over time
   224→
   225→### 3. Session State Integration
   226→
   227→**Concept**: Vault memory bridge + 12D graph
   228→
   229→**Use Case**:
   230→```
   231→Pull session context from vault → Update graph:
   232→- Last commit: "Added 12D projection math" → Highlight mathematical papers
   233→- Active tasks: ["Implement UI controls"] → Suggest UI/UX papers
   234→- Test status: "24/24 passing" → Show green glow on tested components
   235→```
   236→
   237→**Implementation**:
   238→- Read `daily/` session notes via VaultMemoryBridge
   239→- Parse current phase, active tasks, branch name
   240→- Update `dim_agent_journey_affinity` in real-time
   241→- Reflect in graph view (node colors, sizes, clusters)
   242→
   243→### 4. Gap Analysis & Research Suggestions
   244→
   245→**Concept**: Beyond InfraNodus - multi-dimensional gap detection
   246→
   247→**InfraNodus**: Finds missing links between topical clusters
   248→**COHEZION**: Finds gaps across ALL 12 dimensions
   249→
   250→**Example Gaps**:
   251→- **Temporal**: "You have 20 papers from 2025, only 2 from 2024 - missing historical context?"
   252→- **Cross-Domain**: "5 quantum papers, 5 AI papers, 0 bridging them - explore quantum AI?"
   253→- **Conceptual Depth**: "All papers are theory-heavy - add implementation examples?"
   254→- **Citation Impact**: "Paper X cited by 8 others but not linked in graph - create [[concept]] for it?"
   255→
   256→**Implementation (Hybrid AI Architecture)**:
   257→1. **Claude Opus Planning Phase**:
   258→   - Design gap detection strategy: "Analyze 12D topology, what patterns indicate gaps?"
   259→   - Create dimensional analysis prompts for local LLMs
   260→   - Define success criteria for gap identification
   261→
   262→2. **Local LLM Execution Phase**:
   263→   - Process all 84 papers through local embedding model (sentence-transformers)
   264→   - Compute dimensional distributions, detect outliers
   265→   - Identify sparse regions, disconnected clusters
   266→   - Generate candidate gaps (fast, local inference)
   267→
   268→3. **Claude Sonnet Review Phase**:
   269→   - Review local LLM outputs: "Are these gaps meaningful?"
   270→   - Filter false positives, prioritize high-value suggestions
   271→   - Format results for UI display
   272→
   273→4. **Claude Haiku Quick Checks**:
   274→   - Real-time gap checking as user adds papers
   275→   - "Does this new paper fill existing gaps?" (fast, cheap)
   276→
   277→**Cost Optimization**: Opus designs once, local LLMs execute repeatedly, Haiku handles real-time checks
   278→
   279→### 5. Live Dimensional Recomputation
   280→
   281→**Concept**: Dimensions update automatically as vault changes
   282→
   283→**Example**:
   284→```
   285→User edits paper: adds 3 new wiki-links → [[quantum-computing]], [[ai-agents]], [[mcp]]
   286→→ File watcher detects change
   287→→ SurrealDB sync updates paper record
   288→→ Dimensional engine recomputes:
   289→  - dim_connectivity: 5 → 8 (3 new links)
   290→  - dim_cross_domain: 0.2 → 0.7 (now bridges 3 domains)
   291→  - dim_recency: 0.5 → 1.0 (just edited)
   292→→ 3D graph live-updates node position + size
   293→→ User sees change immediately
   294→```
   295→
   296→**Implementation**:
   297→- Watchdog file monitor in SurrealDBSync (✅ already implemented)
   298→- Trigger dimensional recomputation on file changes
   299→- WebSocket push to plugin: "paper X dimensions updated"
   300→- Three.js smooth transition animation (node moves to new position)
   301→

   417→## Technical Stack
   418→
   419→| Component | Technology | Specialist Owner |
   420→|-----------|-----------|------------------|
   421→| **Database** | SurrealDB (native graph) | SurrealDB Specialist ✅ |
   422→| **Sync Layer** | Python + httpx + watchdog | SurrealDB Specialist ✅ |
   423→| **Projection Engine** | TypeScript + math.js | 12D Math Specialist |
   424→| **3D Rendering** | Three.js + WebGL | Plugin Specialist |
   425→| **UI Framework** | Svelte (Obsidian standard) | UI/UX Specialist |
   426→| **AI Orchestration** | Claude Opus (planning) + Sonnet (coordination) + Haiku (quick tasks) | AI Features Specialist |
   427→| **AI Execution** | Local LLMs (Ollama/LM Studio) for inference at scale | AI Features Specialist |
   428→| **Embeddings** | sentence-transformers (local) | AI Features Specialist |
   429→| **Sheets Integration** | Google Sheets API + ADC | Sheets Specialist |
   430→| **Network Analysis** | NetworkX (Python) or custom | AI Features Specialist |
   431→
   432→### AI Model Strategy
   433→
   434→**Claude Models** (via Anthropic API):
   435→- **Opus 4.6**: High-level planning, architectural decisions, complex reasoning
   436→- **Sonnet 4.5**: Implementation coordination, code generation, specialist orchestration
   437→- **Haiku 4.5**: Quick queries, batch processing, simple analysis (1/3 cost, 2x speed)
   438→
   439→**Local LLMs** (via Ollama/LM Studio):
   440→- **Inference at Scale**: Process hundreds of papers for semantic analysis
   441→- **Embedding Generation**: sentence-transformers for similarity computation
   442→- **Gap Analysis Execution**: After Claude Opus designs the strategy, local LLMs execute
   443→- **Real-time Updates**: Fast local inference for live dimensional recomputation
   444→- **Cost Optimization**: Heavy lifting done locally, Claude for orchestration only
   445→

   447→
   448→## Success Metrics
   449→
   450→### Compared to InfraNodus
   451→
   452→| Feature | InfraNodus | COHEZION 12D Graph | Improvement |
   453→|---------|-----------|-------------------|-------------|
   454→| **Dimensions** | 3D (X, Y, Z) | 12D (configurable projection) | **4x richer** |
   455→| **AI Features** | GPT-4 gap analysis | + Agent Journey + Context + Session state | **3x more context** |
   456→| **Real-time Sync** | Manual refresh | Live WebSocket updates | **Instant** |
   457→| **Clustering** | Louvain algorithm | Multi-dimensional + semantic | **Cross-domain detection** |
   458→| **Agent Support** | None | Agent Journey Mode + collaboration tracking | **UNIQUE** |
   459→| **Vault Integration** | Read-only plugin | Bidirectional sync (vault ↔ DB) | **Full integration** |
   460→
   461→### User Experience Targets
   462→
   463→- **Graph load time**: < 2s for 100 papers
   464→- **Projection switch**: < 500ms (instant feel)
   465→- **Live update latency**: < 1s (file save → graph update)
   466→- **AI suggestion generation**: < 5s (GPT-4 call)
   467→- **Node search**: < 100ms (instant filter)
   468→
   469→---
   470→
   471→## Risk Mitigation
   472→
   473→### Technical Risks
   474→
   475→1. **12D → 3D projection loses information**
   476→   - Mitigation: Multiple projection presets, PCA for optimal dimension reduction, user can explore all 12 dimensions via axis mapping
   477→
   478→2. **Performance with large graphs (1000+ nodes)**
   479→   - Mitigation: WebGL instancing, LOD (level of detail), spatial indexing, progressive loading
   480→
   481→3. **SurrealDB WebSocket reliability**
   482→   - Mitigation: Reconnection logic, local caching, optimistic UI updates
   483→
   484→4. **GPT-4 API rate limits**
   485→   - Mitigation: Cache results, batch requests, use Haiku for simple tasks
   486→
   487→### Coordination Risks
   488→
   489→1. **Specialist integration conflicts**
   490→   - Mitigation: Clear API contracts, TypeScript interfaces, weekly sync meetings
   491→
   492→2. **Timeline dependencies (Math → Plugin → UI)**
   493→   - Mitigation: Parallel prototyping, mock implementations, incremental integration
   494→

    44→---
    45→
    46→## Context
    47→
    48→`JourneyTracker` was computing 12D positions by taking the first 12 bytes of `SHA-256(agent_state_string)` and normalizing to floats. This produced positions that were numerically valid and internally consistent — the same state always mapped to the same position — but semantically random.
    49→
    50→Consequences observed:
    51→- Trajectories showed no continuity: semantically adjacent states mapped to distant positions
    52→- Drift detection produced false positives on every step (random walk in 12D)
    53→- Anomaly detection was unusable (everything was anomalous relative to random walk)
    54→- Cluster analysis found no meaningful clusters
    55→
    56→## Decision
    57→
    58→Journey tracking must use FLUME latent vectors as 12D positions. The 12D universe is only meaningful if positions reflect semantic proximity of agent states.
    59→
    60→## Chosen Option
    61→
    62→`agent_state → nomic-embed-text (768D) → FLUME VAE encoder → latent (12D projected)`
    63→
    64→The FLUME latent space becomes the coordinate system for the 12D universe. Semantically similar agent states occupy nearby regions.
    65→
    66→## Alternatives Considered
    67→
    68→1. SHA-256 hash bytes (status quo)
    69→2. Random projection of agent state features
    70→3. PCA of raw state features
    71→4. FLUME semantic embeddings
    72→
    73→## Decision Reasoning
    74→
    75→### Why This Option?
    76→
    77→The entire purpose of the 12D universe is to enable geometric reasoning about agent behavior — drift detection, anomaly identification, trajectory prediction. These require that distance in 12D corresponds to semantic distance in agent reasoning. Only trained semantic embeddings provide this correspondence.
    78→
    79→### Alternatives Rejected
    80→
    81→- **SHA-256** — Avalanche effect makes it semantically meaningless by design.
    82→- **Random projection** — Preserves no semantic structure; positions are arbitrary.
    83→- **PCA** — Captures variance in raw features but not semantic meaning. May work as a fallback if FLUME is unavailable.
    84→
    85→### Confidence Level
    86→
    87→High. The failure of hash-based tracking was directly observed in trajectory visualizations. The fix follows directly from first principles.
    88→
    89→## Expected Outcomes
    90→
    91→- Trajectories show smooth geometric continuity in 12D space
    92→- Drift detection identifies genuine reasoning drift, not random walk
    93→- Cluster analysis reveals meaningful agent behavior modes
    94→- Anomaly detection catches genuine semantic outliers
    95→
    96→## Metrics & Impact
    97→
    98→### Estimated
    99→
   100→- Trajectory smoothness (average step distance) decreases from ~1.4 (random walk expectation in 12D) to <0.3 for coherent agent reasoning
   101→
   102→### Actual (Post-Implementation)
   103→
   104→- Trajectories now show interpretable geometric structure in visualization
   105→

   107→

   109→- [[2026-02-24-anti-pattern-hash-based-journey-tracking-destroys-semantic-meaning]]
   110→- [[agent-journey-tracking]] — journey tracking requires FLUME latent vectors as 12D positions; hash-based positions destroy semantic structure
   111→- [[semantic-search]] — semantic proximity in latent space is the foundation for meaningful journey analysis, just as it is for search
   112→- [[anomaly-detection]] — hash-based trajectories produce false positive anomalies because random walk in 12D is indistinguishable from genuine anomalies
   113→

> AGENT

Now I have the full picture — and it reframes the whole conversation.

The 12D plan treats the SurrealDB layer as "done" (Phase 1 ✅). But what we discovered today is that the foundation still has critical gaps:

- The `neuron` schema has **no `dim_*` fields** yet — the plugin has nowhere to read dimensional data from
- The FLUME embedding pipeline for **Dimension 12** (agent journey affinity) needs the Ollama → nomic-embed-text → 12D projection we have running but haven't wired in
- The plugin connects via **SurrealDB WebSocket LIVE queries** — tested at the DB level but never end-to-end with the plugin
- The "graph architecture improvement" we've been discussing **is the same project** as preparing the 12D data contract

We're not designing two things. The graph architecture improvement **is** the foundation the 12D plugin sits on.

So my recommendation: **scope this spec as the unified graph foundation** — schema + dimensions + MCP tools + annotation API — and treat it as Phase 1.5 before the plugin development begins. It directly unblocks:

1. **Agents** — via MCP graph tools
2. **Humans** — via a 12D plugin that actually has data to render  
3. **Multi-agent coordination** — via the annotation write API

Ready to move to Task 4 and propose 3 approaches for this unified scope? Or is there something about the 12D plan's current state I should understand first?

> AGENT

Here are three meaningfully different approaches. They share the same goal but differ in **what ships first** and **who gets value when**.

---

### Approach A — "MCP Tools First, Dimensions Later"

Build 6-8 graph-aware MCP tools wrapping the existing `fn::context_*` SurrealDB functions. Add the annotation write API. No schema changes yet — 12D fields deferred.

**Ships:** ~1 week  
**New in SurrealDB:** nothing (uses existing stored functions)  
**New in MCP server:** `graph_neighborhood`, `graph_search`, `graph_cluster`, `graph_bridges`, `graph_stats`, `graph_annotate`, `graph_link`  
**Agents get:** native graph tools, no SurrealQL required  
**12D plugin gets:** nothing yet — still needs `dim_*` fields  

**Best if:** the immediate pain is agent operability and the plugin is weeks away.

---

### Approach B — "12D Schema First, MCP Later"

Expand the neuron schema with all 12 `dim_*` fields and compute them during sync. Validate the WebSocket LIVE query path the plugin needs. MCP tools follow after.

**Ships:** ~3-4 weeks  
**New in SurrealDB:** `dim_temporal`, `dim_connectivity`, `dim_depth`, `dim_bridging`, `dim_recency`, `dim_completion` (computable now) + `dim_agent_affinity` (FLUME pipeline: `nomic-embed-text → 768D → 12D projection`)  
**New in sync daemon:** dimension computation on every file change  
**12D plugin gets:** all the data it needs  
**Agents get:** raw access via `surrealdb_query` until MCP tools are added  

**Best if:** the plugin team is ready to start building immediately.

**Risk:** the FLUME embedding pipeline for `dim_agent_affinity` is the hardest part and could block the simpler dimensions.

---

### Approach C — "Parallel Tracks, Converge at Milestone" ⭐ Recommended

Two independent tracks that can run simultaneously and meet at a clean milestone:

**Track 1 — Graph API (~1 week):**  
Extract shared `vault_graph/` Python module used by both the CLI and MCP server. Build 6 MCP graph tools + annotation write API. Both agents and humans get graph access immediately.

**Track 2 — Dimension Expansion (~2 weeks):**  
Add 8 computable `dim_*` fields to the schema (everything except the ML-heavy ones). Compute them in the sync daemon — no FLUME pipeline needed yet. WebSocket LIVE query path validated end-to-end.

**Track 3 — Deferred:**  
`dim_semantic_similarity` and `dim_agent_affinity` (the ones requiring FLUME/nomic embeddings). These need a design conversation of their own. The plugin renders without them — they're enhancements.

**Ships:** Track 1 in ~1 week, both tracks merged in ~2-3 weeks  
**Agents get:** MCP graph tools + annotation API immediately  
**12D plugin gets:** 10 of 12 dimensions within 2-3 weeks  
**Architecture gets:** clean module separation, no code duplication between CLI and MCP  

**Trade-off vs A:** slightly more coordination, but avoids a second schema migration sprint later.  
**Trade-off vs B:** plugin gets 10/12 dimensions instead of all 12, but agents don't wait 3-4 weeks.

---

**My recommendation is C.** Agents are already blocked on the graph being unusable from MCP — that pain is immediate. The plugin team is blocked on missing `dim_*` fields — Track 2 addresses that in parallel. And deferring the FLUME pipeline lets us do that ML work properly without it blocking everything else.

Does C land right, or does the timeline on Track 2 change the calculus?

Presenting the design in sections. First section:

---

## Design Section 1 — Overall Architecture

The system has three layers with clean interfaces between them:

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  CONSUMERS                                                       │
│  Claude (MCP tools)   •   CLI (humans)   •   12D Plugin (WebSocket) │
└──────────────┬──────────────────┬─────────────────┬─────────────┘
               │ MCP calls        │ Python import   │ LIVE queries
┌──────────────▼──────────────────▼─────────────────┼─────────────┐
│  GRAPH API LAYER                                   │             │
│  vault_graph/ (shared Python module)               │             │
│  ├── query.py     — read ops (neighborhood, search, etc.)        │
│  ├── write.py     — annotation writes (activate, stage, link)    │
│  └── dimensions.py — compute dim_* fields                        │
│                                                    │             │
│  MCP tools (6 in server.py) ── import vault_graph ─┤             │
│  CLI (graph_context.py)     ── import vault_graph ─┤             │
└──────────────────────────────────────┬─────────────┼─────────────┘
                                       │ HTTP/SQL    │ WebSocket
┌──────────────────────────────────────▼─────────────▼─────────────┐
│  SURREALDB (canonical truth)                                      │
│  fn::context_* stored functions (existing, expanded)             │
│  neuron schema + 12D dim_* fields (new)                           │
│  vault_sync daemon — computes dimensions on file change           │
└───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Key boundaries:**
- The `vault_graph/` module is the single source of business logic — no duplication between CLI and MCP
- SurrealDB stored functions stay the canonical query layer (agreed coalition)
- The plugin talks **directly to SurrealDB** via WebSocket — it never goes through the MCP server
- Write boundary: sync daemon owns `explicit` synapses + neuron CRUD; agents own `latent`/`dream` synapses + metadata annotations

Does this architecture look right so far?

> DEVELOPER

yes

> AGENT

## Design Section 2 — Track 1: `vault_graph/` Module + MCP Tools + Annotation API

**New module: `scripts/vault_graph/`** (shared by CLI and MCP server)

```
scripts/vault_graph/
├── __init__.py
├── client.py       — SurrealDB HTTP client (extracted from graph_context.py)
├── query.py        — read ops wrapping fn::context_* functions
├── write.py        — annotation write ops
└── dimensions.py   — dimension computation (used by sync daemon)
```

`graph_context.py` becomes a thin CLI wrapper that imports `vault_graph`. The MCP tools import the same module. Zero duplication.

---

**6 new MCP tools in `cloud-vault-mcp/src/mcp_server/server.py`:**

| Tool | Maps to | Purpose |
|------|---------|---------|
| `graph_neighborhood(query)` | `fn::context_neighborhood` | Neuron + all connections |
| `graph_search(query)` | `fn::context_search` | Find neurons by title/tag |
| `graph_cluster(name)` | `fn::context_cluster` | Domain summary + top neurons |
| `graph_bridges(cluster_a, cluster_b)` | `fn::context_bridges` | Cross-domain connectors |
| `graph_stats()` | `fn::vault_stats` | Vault health snapshot |
| `graph_annotate(path, updates)` | `write.py` | Agent annotation writes |

---

**`graph_annotate` write contract** — agents can update:
```python
graph_annotate(
    path="cortex/quantum-foam.md",
    updates={
        "activation": 0.92,          # bump/decay activation
        "stage": "mature",           # embryo → growing → mature → resting
        "tags": ["quantum", "foam"], # replace tag list
        "link_to": "cortex/evo.md", # create latent synapse (link_type: latent)
    }
)
```

**Write rules enforced in `write.py`:**
- Can only set `link_type` to `latent` or `dream` — never `explicit` (sync daemon owns those)
- `stage` can only move forward or to `resting` — no skipping to `mature` from `embryo`
- `activation` clamped to `[0.0, 1.0]`

---

# SurrealDB Labs: Projects & Integrations Overview

## Graph Visualization & Relationships

- **SurrealDB AI Assistant** - Dedicated question-answering tool

## Python Clients

- Multiple Python tutorials (Flask, Streamlit, Django starters)

- **Cerial ORM** - Schema-first, type-safe ORM
- **surrealdb-migrations** - Database schema management
- **SurrealDB Grafana datasource** - Data visualization integration

# SurrealDB MCP Server Overview

## Purpose
This MCP server enables AI assistants to interact with a SurrealDB database through a standardized interface, allowing "query and manipulate data within a configured SurrealDB instance."

## Exposed Tools
The server provides ten primary operations:

- **query**: Execute raw SurrealQL statements
- **select**: Retrieve records from tables (all or by ID)
- **create**: Add new records
- **update**: Replace record content
- **delete**: Remove records by ID
- **merge**: Perform partial updates
- **patch**: Apply JSON Patch operations
- **upsert**: Create or update records
- **insert**: Add multiple records
- **insertRelation**: Create graph edges between records

## Connection Configuration
The server requires five environment variables:

- `SURREALDB_URL`: WebSocket endpoint (e.g., "ws://localhost:8000")
- `SURREALDB_NS`: Target namespace
- `SURREALDB_DB`: Target database
- `SURREALDB_USER`: Authentication username
- `SURREALDB_PASS`: Authentication password

## Integration Support
Installation instructions are provided for Cline, Claude Desktop, GitHub Copilot, Roo Code, Windsurf, and Cursor, using either global npm installation or npx execution.

# GraphRAG Implementation with SurrealDB

## Core Functionality

This implementation combines vector search with graph traversal to enhance LLM responses. The system ingests health symptom data, stores it across two complementary data structures, and uses intelligent querying to provide contextual answers.

## Architecture Components

**Dual Storage Approach:**
The solution maintains both a vector store for semantic similarity matching and a graph store for relationship traversal. As explained, "Graph RAG is an advanced technique. It leverages the structured, interconnected nature of knowledge graphs to provide the LLM with a richer, more contextualised understanding."

**Key Technical Stack:**
- SurrealDBVectorStore with OllamaEmbeddings for semantic search
- SurrealDBGraph for relationship mapping
- LangChain's SurrealDBGraphQAChain for intelligent query generation
- ChatOllama (llama3.2) for natural language processing

## Query Patterns

**Vector Search Phase:**
The system begins by finding relevant symptom documents through similarity search, filtering results to the top matches based on semantic closeness to user input.

**Graph Traversal Phase:**
Once symptoms are identified, the LLM generates SurrealQL graph queries. Example: traversing Treatment→Treats→Symptom relationships to retrieve applicable medical interventions. The generated queries navigate incoming relationships using syntax like `<-relation_Treats<-graph_Treatment`.

This two-stage approach reduces hallucinations by grounding LLM responses in both semantic relevance and explicit relationship data.
```

## Design Section 3 — Track 2: Schema Expansion + Dimension Computation

**8 computable `dim_*` fields added to the `neuron` table** (no ML pipeline needed):

| Field | Type | Computed From | Update Trigger |
|---|---|---|---|
| `dim_temporal` | float 0-1 | `created` date, normalized to vault age | On import |
| `dim_connectivity` | float 0-1 | `synapse_out + synapse_in`, normalized | On synapse change |
| `dim_depth` | float 0-1 | theory/application keyword ratio in content | On file change |
| `dim_recency` | float 0-1 | time-decay from `modified`, half-life 30d | On file change |
| `dim_bridging` | float 0-1 | synapses crossing cluster boundaries / total | On synapse change |
| `dim_completion` | float 0-1 | word count + tags + links present | On file change |
| `dim_domain` | array\<string\> | `cluster_id` + `tags` | On file change |
| `dim_stage` | float 0-1 | embryo=0.25, growing=0.5, mature=0.75, resting=1.0 | On stage change |

**Deferred (Track 3):**
- `dim_semantic_similarity` — requires nomic-embed-text embeddings (Ollama running ✅ but pipeline not wired)
- `dim_agent_affinity` — requires FLUME VAE latent projection (the hash-based approach is already rejected per the lesson learned)

**Dimension computation lives in `scripts/vault_graph/dimensions.py`**, called by the vault sync daemon on every file event. The 12D plugin reads `dim_*` fields directly via WebSocket LIVE query — no MCP hop.

**Schema migration** handled by `surrealdb-migrations` (official tool from Labs) — schema changes tracked as versioned `.surql` files, not applied ad-hoc.

**WebSocket LIVE query validation** — the plugin subscribes like:
```sql
LIVE SELECT id, title, dim_temporal, dim_connectivity, dim_depth,
       dim_recency, dim_bridging, dim_completion, dim_domain, dim_stage
FROM neuron;
```
We validate this end-to-end in Track 2 before handing to the plugin team.

> AGENT

The three-track design is correct but sequenced wrong. Here's the reorder:

**The compound engineering insight:** every token invested in pre-computing intelligence INTO the graph pays compound interest — every future agent that reads that neuron gets the intelligence for free. `dim_agent_affinity` is not a deferred enhancement. It's the multiplier that makes agents compound on each other's work.

**Revised path — "Intelligence Density First":**

---

### Phase A — High-Value Dimensions + Expanded GraphReactor (Week 1)

Drop the urllib HTTP client. Switch to `surrealdb.py` SDK everywhere — better connection handling, proper async, typed results.

Add the three dimensions that carry the most compound value immediately:

| Field | Why it compounds |
|---|---|
| `dim_bridging` | Agents find cross-domain connectors in one read — no traversal needed |
| `dim_completion` | Directs agent effort to where the graph is thin — shared work queue |
| `dim_recency` | Agents know what's live vs stale without reading file timestamps |

**Expand GraphReactor** — currently writes vault health alerts. Now also writes dimensional aggregates into `metabolism/graph-briefing.md`:

```
## Dimensional State
Top bridge neurons: [...] (dim_bridging > 0.8)
Highest completion gaps: [...] (dim_completion < 0.3, activation > 0.7)
Recently active clusters: [...] (avg dim_recency > 0.6)
```

Same ~910 token budget. Richer signal. Every agent at session start gets dimensional awareness for zero extra query cost.

**Register `surrealdb-mcp-server`** (official npm) as a second MCP server alongside `cloud-vault-mcp`. It provides `upsert`, `merge`, `insertRelation` immediately — annotation writes unlocked without building anything.

---

### Phase B — FLUME Pipeline + Agent Trail (Week 2)

Move `dim_agent_affinity` from Track 3 to here. This is the compound engineering unlock:

```
agent task description + active concepts
        ↓
nomic-embed-text (Ollama, running ✅)
        ↓
768D embedding → PCA projection → 12D position
        ↓
written to neuron.dim_agent_affinity (per-session, keyed by agent_id)
        ↓
per-prompt hook reads it — same 275 tokens, now carries affinity signal
```

**The compound effect:** Agent A works on quantum computing → bumps affinity scores on relevant neurons → Agent B spawned later on a related task reads the graph → hot neurons already surfaced → Agent B starts informed, not cold.

The per-prompt hook stays at ~275 tokens but now injects dimensional position alongside connectivity. Not more tokens — more signal per token.

---

### Phase C — `vault_graph/` Module + MCP Graph Tools (Week 2-3)

Now extract the shared module and wrap the 6 MCP graph tools. This is still valuable — agents shouldn't need SurrealQL — but it's not what unlocks compound engineering. It's **leverage** on top of the intelligence that Phase A+B built.

---

### What gets dropped / deferred

- `surrealdb-migrations` for schema versioning — worth using but not blocking
- LangChain `SurrealDBGraphQAChain` — powerful but depends on stable dim_* data first
- `dim_semantic_similarity` — Ollama can compute it but the storage pattern (sparse similarity matrix on every neuron pair) needs careful design to avoid the N² problem

---

**Token efficiency summary:**

| Layer | Before | After | Gain |
|---|---|---|---|
| Session start briefing | Vault health + hot neurons | + dimensional state | Same tokens, richer signal |
| Per-prompt hook | Connectivity only | + agent affinity + bridging | Same 275 tokens |
| Agent annotation writes | Not possible | Via official MCP server | Zero design cost |
| Graph queries from agents | Raw SurrealQL required | 6 MCP tools | Accessible without SurrealQL |

{

  "content": "# Cohezion Graph Architecture — Intelligence Density First\n\n**Date:** 2026-03-20\n**Status:** Approved\n**Authors:** Mike Anderson + Claude Code\n\n---\n\n## 1. Problem Statement\n\nThe vault's SurrealDB graph is a latent asset that isn't delivering value:\n\n- **Agents** have no token-efficient path to graph context at session start\n- **Humans** query the graph with ad-hoc `urllib` HTTP calls (`scripts/graph_context.py`) that bypass the SDK\n- **The 12D visualization plan** (ADR `2026-02-09-12d-graph-refined-plan.md`) is stalled because it has no stable query foundation\n- **Compound engineering** — agents leaving trails that future agents inherit — is architecturally impossible today\n\nThis spec establishes the canonical graph layer that unblocks all three.\n\n---\n\n## 2. Goals\n\n1. **Token-efficient context** — agents get graph orientation in <300 tokens, pre-computed, zero query cost at session start\n2. **Canonical query layer** — one place where graph logic lives (SurrealDB stored functions), thin wrappers everywhere else\n3. **Agentic trails** — agents write `latent` and `dream` synapses + metadata annotations; future agents inherit that signal\n4. **12D plugin foundation** — `dim_*` fields on neurons ready for the visualization layer to consume\n5. **Leverage existing tools** — use SurrealDB Labs components instead of building from scratch\n\n---\n\n## 3. Non-Goals\n\n- Rewriting the Obsidian plugin (that's a separate /spec)\n- Building a new auth/security layer for SurrealDB\n- Replacing the sync daemon's structural write responsibility\n- Full FLUME VAE training pipeline (Phase B uses PCA projection, not full VAE)\n\n---\n\n## 4. Architecture Overview\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                   CONSUMERS                                  │\n│  Claude Code session  │  MCP tools  │  CLI (graph_context)  │\n└───────────┬───────────┴──────┬──────┴──────────┬────────────┘\n            │                  │                  │\n            ▼                  ▼                  ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│              INTELLIGENCE LAYER (canonical)                  │\n│  metabolism/graph-briefing.md  ←  GraphReactor pre-computes │\n│  fn::context_neighborhood      ←  SurrealDB stored functions │\n│  fn::context_search            │                            │\n│  fn::context_cluster           │                            │\n│  fn::context_bridges           │                            │\n│  fn::vault_stats               │                            │\n└──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘\n                               │\n                               ▼\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                   SURREALDB (rocksdb)                        │\n│  neuron { dim_bridging, dim_completion, dim_recency,        │\n│            dim_agent_affinity (Phase B) }                   │\n│  synapse { type: explicit | latent | dream }                │\n└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘\n```\n\n**Key principle:** The stored functions in `scripts/dba/context_functions.surql` are the single source of truth for graph logic. MCP tools and CLI are thin I/O wrappers only.\n\n---\n\n## 5. Write Boundary (Agent vs. Daemon)\n\n| What | Owner | Why |\n|------|-------|-----|\n| `neuron` CRUD (create, update title/tags) | sync daemon | Structural integrity, mirrors vault file state |\n| `synapse` with `type: explicit` | sync daemon | Mirrors Obsidian wiki-links |\n| `synapse` with `type: latent` | agents (via MCP tool) | Semantic inference, not structural |\n| `synapse` with `type: dream` | agents (via MCP tool) | Cross-domain resonance (SurrealDB `dreaming/` layer) |\n| `neuron.dim_agent_affinity` (12D vector) | agents (via MCP tool) | FLUME latent projection of session embedding |\n| `neuron.metadata` annotations | agents (via MCP tool) | `last_accessed`, `access_count`, `agent_notes` |\n\nAgents never overwrite structural fields. The event-sourced upgrade path (append-only `agent_event` table) is deferred to a future spec.\n\n---\n\n## 6. Implementation Phases\n\n### Phase A — Foundation (Week 1)\n\n**Goal:** Every agent session gets pre-computed dimensional context at near-zero token cost.\n\n#### A1: SDK Migration\n\nReplace `urllib` HTTP calls in `scripts/graph_context.py` with `surrealdb.py` SDK.\n\n- Install: `uv add surrealdb` in `cloud-vault-mcp`\n- Connection: reuse existing `SURREALDB_URL`, `SURREALDB_USERNAME`, `SURREALDB_PASSWORD` env vars\n- Keep existing CLI surface (`briefing`, `neighborhood`, `search`, etc.) — only internals change\n- Error handling: raise `GraphQueryError` with the original SurrealDB error message; let callers decide retry logic\n\n#### A2: Schema Dimensions\n\nAdd three dimension fields to the `neuron` table (migration via `surrealdb-migrations`):\n\n```surql\nDEFINE FIELD dim_bridging   ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- betweenness centrality 0-1\nDEFINE FIELD dim_completion ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- note completeness score 0-1\nDEFINE FIELD dim_recency    ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- recency decay 0-1\n```\n\nComputation:\n- `dim_bridging`: run betweenness centrality on synapse graph; normalize 0-1\n- `dim_completion`: word count + frontmatter completeness heuristic; normalize 0-1\n- `dim_recency`: `exp(-λ * days_since_modified)` with λ = 0.05 (half-life ~14 days)\n\nGraphReactor computes these on file-change events and writes them via `surrealdb.py`.\n\n#### A3: GraphReactor Expansion\n\nExtend `scripts/graph_reactor.py` to write dimensional aggregates into `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` on each change cycle.\n\nBriefing template (~275 tokens):\n\n```markdown\n## Graph State — {date}\n\n**Vault:** {neuron_count} neurons · {synapse_count} synapses\n\n**Hot neurons** (high bridging):\n{top_5_bridging_neurons_with_titles}\n\n**Bridges** (connect otherwise-disconnected clusters):\n{top_3_bridge_neurons}\n\n**Completion gaps** (low dim_completion, high dim_bridging):\n{top_3_stubs_worth_fleshing_out}\n\n**Recent activity** (high dim_recency):\n{top_5_recently_modified}\n```\n\nThe per-prompt hook (`scripts/hooks/graph_context_hook.py`) already injects this file. No hook changes needed in Phase A.\n\n#### A4: Official SurrealDB MCP Server\n\nRegister `nsxdavid/surrealdb-mcp-server` as a second MCP server in `~/.claude/mcp.json`:\n\n```json\n\"surrealdb\": {\n  \"command\": \"npx\",\n  \"args\": [\"-y\", \"surrealdb-mcp-server\"],\n  \"env\": {\n    \"SURREALDB_URL\": \"http://localhost:8001\",\n    \"SURREALDB_USER\": \"sdb_admin_session43\",\n    \"SURREALDB_PASS\": \"<from service file>\",\n    \"SURREALDB_NS\": \"cohezion\",\n    \"SURREALDB_DB\": \"vault\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThis gives Claude Code direct SurrealQL execution capability without custom code.\n\n---\n\n### Phase B — Compound Trails (Week 2)\n\n**Goal:** Agents leave a latent affinity signal that future agents inherit, creating compounding intelligence.\n\n#### B1: FLUME Affinity Pipeline\n\nFor each agent session, compute `dim_agent_affinity` (12D vector):\n\n```\nsession_text → nomic-embed-text (768D) → PCA projection (12D) → store on accessed neurons\n```\n\n- Use Ollama's `nomic-embed-text` model (already in vault stack)\n- PCA matrix: pre-computed from existing neuron corpus, stored in `scripts/dba/pca_matrix.npy`\n- PCA refit trigger: when neuron count grows by >10% since last fit\n- Write path: `vault_graph.write_agent_affinity(neuron_id, vec_12d)` → `UPDATE neuron SET dim_agent_affinity = $vec WHERE id = $id`\n\n**Critical constraint from ADR `2026-02-23-hash-based-journey-tracking`:** hash-based approach is rejected. Only embedding-based projection is canonical.\n\n#### B2: Hook Enrichment\n\nExtend `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` to include affinity signal:\n\n```markdown\n**Agent trails** (neurons with high agent affinity in this domain):\n{top_3_by_dim_agent_affinity_cosine_similarity_to_current_session}\n```\n\nThe hook now provides ~350 tokens total — still well within the target budget.\n\n#### B3: Agent-Write MCP Tools\n\nAdd four tools to the existing `cloud-vault-mcp` server:\n\n| Tool | Description |\n|------|-------------|\n| `graph_write_latent_synapse` | Create `type: latent` synapse between two neurons |\n| `graph_write_dream_synapse` | Create `type: dream` synapse (cross-domain resonance) |\n| `graph_write_affinity` | Write 12D affinity vector to neuron |\n| `graph_annotate_neuron` | Write metadata annotations (last_accessed, agent_notes) |\n\nAll four tools validate that the caller is not overwriting `type: explicit` synapses or structural neuron fields.\n\n---\n\n### Phase C — Module Extraction (Weeks 2-3)\n\n**Goal:** Clean architecture — graph logic lives in one importable module.\n\n#### C1: `vault_graph/` Module\n\nExtract all graph query logic from `scripts/graph_context.py` into `src/mcp_server/vault_graph/`:\n\n```\nvault_graph/\n├── __init__.py\n├── client.py        # surrealdb.py connection + auth\n├── queries.py       # thin wrappers around stored functions\n├── reactor.py       # GraphReactor (file-change → dimension update → briefing write)\n├── affinity.py      # FLUME PCA pipeline\n└── tools.py         # MCP tool implementations (calls queries.py)\n```\n\n#### C2: Six Read MCP Tools\n\nWrap existing stored functions as MCP tools:\n\n| Tool | Stored Function | Description |\n|------|-----------------|-------------|\n| `graph_neighborhood` | `fn::context_neighborhood` | N-hop neighbors of a neuron |\n| `graph_search` | `fn::context_search` | Semantic full-text search |\n| `graph_cluster` | `fn::context_cluster` | Cluster a neuron's neighborhood |\n| `graph_hops` | `fn::context_hops` | Shortest path between two neurons |\n| `graph_bridges` | `fn::context_bridges` | Neurons bridging disconnected clusters |\n| `graph_stats` | `fn::vault_stats` | Global vault statistics |\n\nThese replace the current curl-based tooling. The CLI (`scripts/graph_context.py`) becomes a thin wrapper that calls `vault_graph.queries` instead of raw HTTP.\n\n---\n\n## 7. Error Handling\n\n| Failure | Behavior |\n|---------|----------|\n| SurrealDB unreachable at session start | Hook returns empty briefing (graceful degradation), logs warning |\n| stored function returns empty result | Return empty list, never raise; callers handle optional results |\n| `dim_agent_affinity` write fails | Log warning, continue session — affinity is additive, not critical |\n| PCA matrix missing | Skip affinity computation for that session, log warning |\n| Migration fails | Roll back via `surrealdb-migrations` rollback command, surface error |\n\n---\n\n## 8. Testing Strategy\n\n| Layer | How |\n|-------|-----|\n| Stored functions | `scripts/dba/test_functions.surql` — run against test namespace |\n| `vault_graph/` module | pytest with `surrealdb.py` test client against ephemeral SurrealDB instance |\n| MCP tools | Integration tests via `mcp[cli]` test harness |\n| GraphReactor | File-change event simulation; assert `graph-briefing.md` is updated |\n| FLUME pipeline | Unit test PCA projection with fixed seed; assert output is 12D |\n| Per-prompt hook | Assert briefing injected in <300 tokens for typical vault size |\n\n---\n\n## 9. Dependencies\n\n| Component | Source | Notes |\n|-----------|--------|-------|\n| `surrealdb` Python SDK | `uv add surrealdb` | Replaces `urllib` HTTP calls |\n| `surrealdb-mcp-server` | npm: `nsxdavid/surrealdb-mcp-server` | Direct SurrealQL from Claude Code |\n| `surrealdb-migrations` | cargo or npm | Schema migration management |\n| `nomic-embed-text` | Ollama (already running) | Session embedding for FLUME |\n| `numpy` | already in env | PCA matrix operations |\n\n---\n\n## 10. Key Files Affected\n\n| File | Change |\n|------|--------|\n| `scripts/graph_context.py` | Replace urllib with surrealdb.py SDK; keep CLI surface |\n| `scripts/graph_reactor.py` | Add dim_bridging/completion/recency computation; expand briefing template |\n| `scripts/dba/triune-schema.surql` | Add `dim_*` field definitions |\n| `scripts/dba/context_functions.surql` | No changes — these are the canonical layer |\n| `src/mcp_server/vault_graph/` | New module (Phase C) |\n| `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` | Template expanded (auto-generated, not hand-edited) |\n| `~/.claude/mcp.json` | Add surrealdb-mcp-server entry |\n\n---\n\n## 11. Success Criteria\n\n- [ ] Agent sessions get graph briefing in <300 tokens with zero query cost\n- [ ] `dim_bridging`, `dim_completion`, `dim_recency` populated on all existing neurons\n- [ ] Agent writes latent/dream synapse via MCP tool without touching explicit synapses\n- [ ] `dim_agent_affinity` vectors written after session accessing neurons\n- [ ] `vault_graph/` module has >80% test coverage\n- [ ] All six read MCP tools return results matching direct stored function calls\n"
}
```

```
     1→# Cohezion Graph Architecture — Intelligence Density First
     2→
     3→**Date:** 2026-03-20
     4→**Status:** Approved
     5→**Authors:** Mike Anderson + Claude Code
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## 1. Problem Statement
    10→
    11→The vault's SurrealDB graph is a latent asset that isn't delivering value:
    12→
    13→- **Agents** have no token-efficient path to graph context at session start
    14→- **Humans** query the graph with ad-hoc `urllib` HTTP calls (`scripts/graph_context.py`) that bypass the SDK
    15→- **The 12D visualization plan** (ADR `2026-02-09-12d-graph-refined-plan.md`) is stalled because it has no stable query foundation
    16→- **Compound engineering** — agents leaving trails that future agents inherit — is architecturally impossible today
    17→
    18→This spec establishes the canonical graph layer that unblocks all three.
    19→
    20→---
    21→
    22→## 2. Goals
    23→
    24→1. **Token-efficient context** — agents get graph orientation in <300 tokens, pre-computed, zero query cost at session start
    25→2. **Canonical query layer** — one place where graph logic lives (SurrealDB stored functions), thin wrappers everywhere else
    26→3. **Agentic trails** — agents write `latent` and `dream` synapses + metadata annotations; future agents inherit that signal
    27→4. **12D plugin foundation** — `dim_*` fields on neurons ready for the visualization layer to consume
    28→5. **Leverage existing tools** — use SurrealDB Labs components instead of building from scratch
    29→
    30→---
    31→
    32→## 3. Non-Goals
    33→
    34→- Rewriting the Obsidian plugin (that's a separate /spec)
    35→- Building a new auth/security layer for SurrealDB
    36→- Replacing the sync daemon's structural write responsibility
    37→- Full FLUME VAE training pipeline (Phase B uses PCA projection, not full VAE)
    38→
    39→---
    40→
    41→## 4. Architecture Overview
    42→
    43→```
    44→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    45→│                   CONSUMERS                                  │
    46→│  Claude Code session  │  MCP tools  │  CLI (graph_context)  │
    47→└───────────┬───────────┴──────┬──────┴──────────┬────────────┘
    48→            │                  │                  │
    49→            ▼                  ▼                  ▼
    50→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    51→│              INTELLIGENCE LAYER (canonical)                  │
    52→│  metabolism/graph-briefing.md  ←  GraphReactor pre-computes │
    53→│  fn::context_neighborhood      ←  SurrealDB stored functions │
    54→│  fn::context_search            │                            │
    55→│  fn::context_cluster           │                            │
    56→│  fn::context_bridges           │                            │
    57→│  fn::vault_stats               │                            │
    58→└──────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────┘
    59→                               │
    60→                               ▼
    61→┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
    62→│                   SURREALDB (rocksdb)                        │
    63→│  neuron { dim_bridging, dim_completion, dim_recency,        │
    64→│            dim_agent_affinity (Phase B) }                   │
    65→│  synapse { type: explicit | latent | dream }                │
    66→└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    67→```
    68→
    69→**Key principle:** The stored functions in `scripts/dba/context_functions.surql` are the single source of truth for graph logic. MCP tools and CLI are thin I/O wrappers only.
    70→
    71→---
    72→
    73→## 5. Write Boundary (Agent vs. Daemon)
    74→
    75→| What | Owner | Why |
    76→|------|-------|-----|
    77→| `neuron` CRUD (create, update title/tags) | sync daemon | Structural integrity, mirrors vault file state |
    78→| `synapse` with `type: explicit` | sync daemon | Mirrors Obsidian wiki-links |
    79→| `synapse` with `type: latent` | agents (via MCP tool) | Semantic inference, not structural |
    80→| `synapse` with `type: dream` | agents (via MCP tool) | Cross-domain resonance (SurrealDB `dreaming/` layer) |
    81→| `neuron.dim_agent_affinity` (12D vector) | agents (via MCP tool) | FLUME latent projection of session embedding |
    82→| `neuron.metadata` annotations | agents (via MCP tool) | `last_accessed`, `access_count`, `agent_notes` |
    83→
    84→Agents never overwrite structural fields. The event-sourced upgrade path (append-only `agent_event` table) is deferred to a future spec.
    85→
    86→---
    87→
    88→## 6. Implementation Phases
    89→
    90→### Phase A — Foundation (Week 1)
    91→
    92→**Goal:** Every agent session gets pre-computed dimensional context at near-zero token cost.
    93→
    94→#### A1: SDK Migration
    95→
    96→Replace `urllib` HTTP calls in `scripts/graph_context.py` with `surrealdb.py` SDK.
    97→
    98→- Install: `uv add surrealdb` in `cloud-vault-mcp`
    99→- Connection: reuse existing `SURREALDB_URL`, `SURREALDB_USERNAME`, `SURREALDB_PASSWORD` env vars
   100→- Keep existing CLI surface (`briefing`, `neighborhood`, `search`, etc.) — only internals change
   101→- Error handling: raise `GraphQueryError` with the original SurrealDB error message; let callers decide retry logic
   102→
   103→#### A2: Schema Dimensions
   104→
   105→Add three dimension fields to the `neuron` table (migration via `surrealdb-migrations`):
   106→
   107→```surql
   108→DEFINE FIELD dim_bridging   ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- betweenness centrality 0-1
   109→DEFINE FIELD dim_completion ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- note completeness score 0-1
   110→DEFINE FIELD dim_recency    ON neuron TYPE option<float>;  -- recency decay 0-1
   111→```
   112→
   113→Computation:
   114→- `dim_bridging`: run betweenness centrality on synapse graph; normalize 0-1
   115→- `dim_completion`: word count + frontmatter completeness heuristic; normalize 0-1
   116→- `dim_recency`: `exp(-λ * days_since_modified)` with λ = 0.05 (half-life ~14 days)
   117→
   118→GraphReactor computes these on file-change events and writes them via `surrealdb.py`.
   119→
   120→#### A3: GraphReactor Expansion
   121→
   122→Extend `scripts/graph_reactor.py` to write dimensional aggregates into `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` on each change cycle.
   123→
   124→Briefing template (~275 tokens):
   125→
   126→```markdown
   127→## Graph State — {date}
   128→
   129→**Vault:** {neuron_count} neurons · {synapse_count} synapses
   130→
   131→**Hot neurons** (high bridging):
   132→{top_5_bridging_neurons_with_titles}
   133→
   134→**Bridges** (connect otherwise-disconnected clusters):
   135→{top_3_bridge_neurons}
   136→
   137→**Completion gaps** (low dim_completion, high dim_bridging):
   138→{top_3_stubs_worth_fleshing_out}
   139→
   140→**Recent activity** (high dim_recency):
   141→{top_5_recently_modified}
   142→```
   143→
   144→The per-prompt hook (`scripts/hooks/graph_context_hook.py`) already injects this file. No hook changes needed in Phase A.
   145→
   146→#### A4: Official SurrealDB MCP Server
   147→
   148→Register `nsxdavid/surrealdb-mcp-server` as a second MCP server in `~/.claude/mcp.json`.
   149→
   150→> **Package name:** The npm package name must be confirmed before implementation — try `npx -y surrealdb-mcp-server` and fall back to `npx -y github:nsxdavid/surrealdb-mcp-server` if the npm registry name differs.
   151→
   152→```json
   153→"surrealdb": {
   154→  "command": "npx",
   155→  "args": ["-y", "surrealdb-mcp-server"],
   156→  "env": {
   157→    "SURREALDB_URL": "http://localhost:8001",
   158→    "SURREALDB_USER": "sdb_admin_session43",
   159→    "SURREALDB_PASS": "<from service file>",
   160→    "SURREALDB_NS": "cohezion",
   161→    "SURREALDB_DB": "vault"
   162→  }
   163→}
   164→```
   165→
   166→This gives Claude Code direct SurrealQL execution capability without custom code.
   167→
   168→---
   169→
   170→### Phase B — Compound Trails (Week 2)
   171→
   172→**Goal:** Agents leave a latent affinity signal that future agents inherit, creating compounding intelligence.
   173→
   174→#### B1: FLUME Affinity Pipeline
   175→
   176→For each agent session, compute `dim_agent_affinity` (12D vector):
   177→
   178→```
   179→session_text → nomic-embed-text (768D) → PCA projection (12D) → store on accessed neurons
   180→```
   181→
   182→- Use Ollama's `nomic-embed-text` model (already in vault stack)
   183→- PCA matrix: pre-computed from existing neuron corpus, stored in `scripts/dba/pca_matrix.npy`
   184→- PCA refit trigger: when neuron count grows by >10% since last fit
   185→- Write path: `vault_graph.write_agent_affinity(neuron_id, vec_12d)` → `UPDATE neuron SET dim_agent_affinity = $vec WHERE id = $id`
   186→
   187→**Critical constraint from ADR `2026-02-23-hash-based-journey-tracking`:** hash-based approach is rejected. Only embedding-based projection is canonical.
   188→
   189→#### B2: Hook Enrichment
   190→
   191→Extend `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` to include affinity signal:
   192→
   193→```markdown
   194→**Agent trails** (neurons with high agent affinity in this domain):
   195→{top_3_by_dim_agent_affinity_cosine_similarity_to_current_session}
   196→```
   197→
   198→The hook now provides ~350 tokens total — still well within the target budget.
   199→
   200→#### B3: Agent-Write MCP Tools
   201→
   202→Add four tools to the existing `cloud-vault-mcp` server. These tool stubs call `scripts/graph_context.py` (pre-extraction) and are rewired to `vault_graph/` when Phase C completes — this is an explicit two-step, not a contradiction.
   203→
   204→| Tool | Description |
   205→|------|-------------|
   206→| `graph_write_latent_synapse` | Create `type: latent` synapse between two neurons |
   207→| `graph_write_dream_synapse` | Create `type: dream` synapse (cross-domain resonance) |
   208→| `graph_write_affinity` | Write 12D affinity vector to neuron |
   209→| `graph_annotate_neuron` | Write metadata annotations (last_accessed, agent_notes) |
   210→
   211→All four tools validate that the caller is not overwriting `type: explicit` synapses or structural neuron fields.
   212→
   213→---
   214→
   215→### Phase C — Module Extraction (Weeks 2-3)
   216→
   217→**Goal:** Clean architecture — graph logic lives in one importable module.
   218→
   219→#### C1: `vault_graph/` Module
   220→
   221→Extract all graph query logic from `scripts/graph_context.py` into `src/mcp_server/vault_graph/`.
   222→
   223→> **Package root:** `cloud-vault-mcp` is the project at `~/dev/cohezion/cloud-vault-mcp`; its package root is `src/mcp_server/` (per `pyproject.toml`: `packages = ["src/mcp_server"]`). These are the same thing — `vault_graph/` lives at `src/mcp_server/vault_graph/` inside that repo.
   224→
   225→```
   226→vault_graph/
   227→├── __init__.py
   228→├── client.py        # surrealdb.py connection + auth
   229→├── queries.py       # thin wrappers around stored functions
   230→├── reactor.py       # GraphReactor (file-change → dimension update → briefing write)
   231→├── affinity.py      # FLUME PCA pipeline
   232→└── tools.py         # MCP tool implementations (calls queries.py)
   233→```
   234→
   235→#### C2: Six Read MCP Tools
   236→
   237→Wrap existing stored functions as MCP tools:
   238→
   239→| Tool | Stored Function | Description |
   240→|------|-----------------|-------------|
   241→| `graph_neighborhood` | `fn::context_neighborhood` | N-hop neighbors of a neuron |
   242→| `graph_search` | `fn::context_search` | Semantic full-text search |
   243→| `graph_cluster` | `fn::context_cluster` | Cluster a neuron's neighborhood |
   244→| `graph_hops` | `fn::context_hops` | Shortest path between two neurons |
   245→| `graph_bridges` | `fn::context_bridges` | Neurons bridging disconnected clusters |
   246→| `graph_stats` | `fn::vault_stats` | Global vault statistics |
   247→
   248→These replace the current curl-based tooling. The CLI (`scripts/graph_context.py`) becomes a thin wrapper that calls `vault_graph.queries` instead of raw HTTP.
   249→
   250→---
   251→
   252→## 7. Error Handling
   253→
   254→| Failure | Behavior |
   255→|---------|----------|
   256→| SurrealDB unreachable at session start | Hook returns empty briefing (graceful degradation), logs warning |
   257→| stored function returns empty result | Return empty list, never raise; callers handle optional results |
   258→| `dim_agent_affinity` write fails | Log warning, continue session — affinity is additive, not critical |
   259→| PCA matrix missing | Skip affinity computation for that session, log warning |
   260→| Migration fails | Roll back via `surrealdb-migrations` rollback command, surface error |
   261→
   262→---
   263→
   264→## 8. Testing Strategy
   265→
   266→| Layer | How |
   267→|-------|-----|
   268→| Stored functions | `scripts/dba/test_functions.surql` — run against test namespace |
   269→| `vault_graph/` module | pytest with `surrealdb.py` test client against ephemeral SurrealDB instance |
   270→| MCP tools | Integration tests via `mcp[cli]` test harness |
   271→| GraphReactor | File-change event simulation; assert `graph-briefing.md` is updated |
   272→| FLUME pipeline | Unit test PCA projection with fixed seed; assert output is 12D |
   273→| Per-prompt hook | Assert briefing injected in <300 tokens for typical vault size |
   274→
   275→---
   276→
   277→## 9. Dependencies
   278→
   279→| Component | Source | Notes |
   280→|-----------|--------|-------|
   281→| `surrealdb` Python SDK | `uv add surrealdb` | Replaces `urllib` HTTP calls |
   282→| `surrealdb-mcp-server` | npm: `nsxdavid/surrealdb-mcp-server` | Direct SurrealQL from Claude Code |
   283→| `surrealdb-migrations` | cargo or npm | Schema migration management |
   284→| `nomic-embed-text` | Ollama (already running) | Session embedding for FLUME |
   285→| `numpy` | already in env | PCA matrix operations |
   286→
   287→---
   288→
   289→## 10. Key Files Affected
   290→
   291→| File | Change |
   292→|------|--------|
   293→| `scripts/graph_context.py` | Replace urllib with surrealdb.py SDK; keep CLI surface |
   294→| `scripts/graph_reactor.py` | Add dim_bridging/completion/recency computation; expand briefing template |
   295→| `scripts/dba/triune-schema.surql` | Add `dim_*` field definitions |
   296→| `scripts/dba/context_functions.surql` | No changes — these are the canonical layer |
   297→| `src/mcp_server/vault_graph/` | New module (Phase C) |
   298→| `metabolism/graph-briefing.md` | Template expanded (auto-generated, not hand-edited) |
   299→| `~/.claude/mcp.json` | Add surrealdb-mcp-server entry |
   300→
   301→---
   302→
   303→## 11. Success Criteria
   304→
   305→- [ ] Agent sessions get graph briefing in <300 tokens with zero query cost
   306→- [ ] `dim_bridging`, `dim_completion`, `dim_recency` populated on all existing neurons
   307→- [ ] Agent writes latent/dream synapse via MCP tool without touching explicit synapses
   308→- [ ] `dim_agent_affinity` vectors written after session accessing neurons
   309→- [ ] `vault_graph/` module has >80% test coverage
   310→- [ ] All six read MCP tools return results matching direct stored function calls
   311→
